\displaystyle=DC_{1}(t)F_{1}\bigg(Kp(t)^{2}+\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{D-K}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}=DC_{1}(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}.

where the last equality holds by Lemma
F.1
.
Plugging the calculation results for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
into (
D.1
), we can immediately derive (
D.13
), which, as we stated previously, directly conclude (
D.11
). In addition, we can further calculate that

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=C_{1}(t+1)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}=\Bigg(C_{1}(t)+D\eta\bigg(\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}-C_{1}(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\bigg)\Bigg)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},

which finishes the proof of (
D.12
). Next, we prove that
C_{1}(t)
is always non-negative by induction. Obviously
C_{1}(t)\geq 0
, and we prove that
C_{1}(t+1)\geq 0
by assuming that
C_{1}(t)\geq 0
. Firstly, we define that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)=\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)F_{1}^{(t)}}.

Then based on the definition of
C_{1}^{*}(t)
, the iterative rule for
C_{1}(t)
can be re-written as

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)=C_{1}(t)+\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\bigg(1-\frac{C_{1}(t)}{C_{1}^{*}(t)}\bigg).

From the iterative rule above, it is clear that if
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
, then
C_{1}(t+1)\geq C_{1}(t)
, and
C_{1}(t+1)<C_{1}(t)
if
C_{1}(t)>C_{1}^{*}(t)
. Notice that Lemma
D.3
immediately implies that
1\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}
. We can conclude that once
C_{1}(t)
surpasses
\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}
, then it starts to decrease until it becomes lower than
\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}+D\eta\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}+D\eta\frac{Kp(t)^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{Kp(t)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}+\eta D\bigg(p(t)+\sqrt{\frac{1}{K(D-K)}}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\bigg)\leq\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}+\frac{2\eta D}{K}\leq\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}},

where the second inequality holds as
F_{3}^{(t)}\leq Kp(t)^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)
demonstrated in Lemma
F.5
, and the last inequality holds by the condition of
\eta
that
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}(D^{-5/2})
in Theorem
3.1
. Now we prove that
C_{1}(t+1)\geq 0
holds for both cases:
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
and
C_{1}(t)>C_{1}^{*}(t)
. If
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
, then it is straightforward that
C_{1}(t+1)\geq C_{1}(t)\geq 0
. If
C_{1}(t)>C_{1}^{*}(t)
, then we have

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}(t)-\eta DC_{1}(t)F_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}(t)-\frac{D\eta C_{1}(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}{D-K}

\displaystyle\geq 1-D\eta\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}\frac{\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}{D-K}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}\frac{D}{K}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Here, the second inequality holds as
F_{1}^{(t)}\leq\frac{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}{D-K}
implied by Lemma
F.1
.
The second inequality holds by
C_{1}(t)\leq\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}
, and
C_{1}(t)\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)\geq 1
. The third inequality holds as
DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\leq\frac{D-K}{K}
when
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t)\leq\frac{1}{K}
.
The last inequality holds by the condition of
\eta
that
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}(D^{-5/2})
in Theorem
3.1
. This finishes the proof that
C_{1}(t)
is always non-negative.
∎

In the proof above, we introduce the definition of a proxy
C_{1}^{*}(t)=\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)F_{1}^{(t)}}
, and utilize this proxy to provide an upper bound for
C_{1}(t)
. In fact,
C_{1}^{*}(t)
can be regarded as a “stationary point” of the iterative rule for
C_{1}(t)
in (
D.12
). Inspired by the proof techniques proposed in
56
, we introduce the following lemma, which offers a more refined upper bound for
C_{1}(t)
. We demonstrate this lemma prior to Lemma
D.8
, as its conclusion will be utilized in the proof of Lemma
D.8
.

Lemma D.5
.

Suppose all conditions of Theorem
3.1
hold, and
C_{1}(t)
,
C_{1}^{*}(t)
are as defined in Lemma
D.4
. In addition, define that

\displaystyle A(t)=\begin{cases}Kp(t)^{2}&\text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is identity map};\\
\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{4}+\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{2\pi}\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\Big)&\text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is ReLU activation function};\\
\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}Kp(t)^{2}}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}Kp(t)^{2}}{2\pi}\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\Big)&\text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is Leaky ReLU activation function},\end{cases}

(D.15)

and

\displaystyle B(t)=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is identity map};\\
\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)&\text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is ReLU activation function};\\
\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)&\text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is Leaky ReLU activation function}.\end{cases}

(D.16)

Then it always holds that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)\leq\bigg(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5\big(A(t)+B(t)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t),

(D.17)

Specifically, when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
, this upper bound can be tighter as
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
.

Remark D.6
.

In fact, by checking the definition of
F_{3}^{(t)}
in Lemma
D.2
and its calculated value in Lemma
F.5
, we can conclude that
F_{3}^{(t)}=A(t)+B(t)
.

In addition, we also have the following lemma, which provides further calculation results when the conclusion of Lemma
D.5
holds. This result will be utilized in the proof of Lemma
D.8
.

Lemma D.7
.

Suppose
C_{1}(t)
,
C_{1}^{*}(t)
as defined in Lemma
D.4
, and satisfying that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)=\bigg(1+\alpha\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)

for some scalar
\alpha<1
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-KC_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)=\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}(1-\alpha)A(t);

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}-C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)=\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}(1-\alpha)A(t).

Proof of Lemma
D.7
.

We prove this lemma when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, ReLU activation function, and Leaky ReLU activation function, respectively.
When
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map,
utilizing Lemma
F.1
, Lemma
F.2
, Lemma
F.5
, Lemma
F.6
, and Lemma
F.7
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle F_{1}^{(t)}=Kp(t)^{2}+\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{D-K};\quad F_{2,1}^{(t)}=p(t)^{2};\quad F_{2,2}^{(t)}=\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}};

\displaystyle F_{3}^{(t)}=Kp(t)^{2};\quad F_{4}^{(t)}=p(t)^{2};\quad F_{5}^{(t)}=0.

(D.18)

Then combined with the definition of
A(t)
,
B(t)
in Lemma
D.5
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-KC_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-\bigg(1+\alpha\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)KC_{1}^{*}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)}A(t)-\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}A(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}(1-\alpha)A(t).

where the first inequality holds by applying
C_{1}(t)=\big(1+\alpha\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
and
B(t)=0
, the second inequality holds by applying the definition of
C_{1}^{*}(t)
and the calculation results illustrated in (
D.1
). Similarly, we can also derive that

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}-C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A(t)}{Kp(t)}-\bigg(1+\alpha\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\frac{Dp(t)-1}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}A(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}(1-\alpha)A(t).

This finishes the proof when
\sigma(\cdot)
is identity map. When
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, utilizing Lemma
F.1
, Lemma
F.2
, Lemma
F.5
, Lemma
F.6
, and Lemma
F.7
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle F_{1}^{(t)}=\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{2}+\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{2(D-K)};\quad F_{2,1}^{(t)}=\frac{p(t)^{2}}{2};\quad F_{2,2}^{(t)}=\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{2(D-K)^{2}};

\displaystyle F_{3}^{(t)}=\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{4}+\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)=A(t)+B(t);

\displaystyle F_{4}^{(t)}=\frac{p(t)^{2}}{4}+\frac{p(t)^{2}}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)^{3}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{2\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad=A(t)+\frac{(D-K)p(t)^{2}}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}B(t);

\displaystyle F_{5}^{(t)}=\frac{p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{3}}{2\pi(D-K)\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}.

(D.19)

Then combined with the definition of
A(t)
,
B(t)
in Lemma
D.5
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-KC_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\frac{C_{1}^{*}(t)Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{2(D-K)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)}A(t)-\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}A(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{(1\!-\!Kp(t))\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}{DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1}B(t)\!-\!\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)\frac{(1\!-\!Kp(t))\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}{DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1}B(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}\Bigg(\bigg(1-\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\bigg)A(t)-\frac{\alpha A(t)B(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}(1-\alpha)A(t).

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}-C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}-\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}B(t)

\displaystyle-\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)\frac{F_{1}^{(t)}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(t)}-\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\frac{Dp(t)-1}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}\Bigg)A(t)

\displaystyle+\Bigg[\frac{1}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}\!

\displaystyle-\!\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)\frac{Dp(t)-1}{DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1}\Bigg]B(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}\Bigg(\bigg(1-\frac{\alpha A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\bigg)A(t)-\frac{\alpha A(t)B(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}(1-\alpha)A(t).

This completes the proof of the scenario that
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function. For the case that
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, utilizing Lemma
F.1
, Lemma
F.2
, Lemma
F.5
, Lemma
F.6
, and Lemma
F.7
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle F_{1}^{(t)}=\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})Kp(t)^{2}}{2}+\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{2(D-K)};

\displaystyle F_{2,1}^{(t)}=\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})p(t)^{2}}{2};\quad F_{2,2}^{(t)}=\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{2(D-K)^{2}};

\displaystyle F_{3}^{(t)}=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}Kp(t)^{2}}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}Kp(t)^{2}}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)=A(t)+B(t);

\displaystyle F_{4}^{(t)}=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}p(t)^{2}}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}p(t)^{2}}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)^{3}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{2\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)}=A(t)+\frac{(D-K)p(t)^{2}}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}B(t);

\displaystyle F_{5}^{(t)}=\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{3}}{2\pi(D-K)\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}.

(D.20)

Then the remaining proof is entirely identical to that of the ReLU activation function, when replacing the values of these terms demonstrated in (
D.1
).
∎

Based on the conclusion of Lemma
D.5
and Lemma
D.7
, we are now prepared to prove Lemma
D.8
. We will address the proof of Lemma
D.5
after completing the proof of Lemma
D.8
.

Lemma D.8
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
3.1
, there exist time dependent non-negative, monotonically increasing scalars
C_{2}(t)
and
C_{3}(t)
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}=C_{2}(t)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}-C_{3}(t)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}.

(D.21)

Due to the specific pattern of
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}
demonstrated in (
D.21
), there exist a time dependent scalar
p(t)
, such that
\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}=p(t)
for all
i\in[D]
and
i_{1}\in G^{i}
. Otherwise,
\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}=\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}
. Additionally,
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t)\leq\frac{1}{K}
and
p(t)
is monotonically increasing.
Based on the definition of
p(t)
,
C_{2}(t)
and
C_{3}(t)
have the following iterative rules

\displaystyle C_{2}(t+1)=

\displaystyle C_{2}(t)+\eta\frac{C_{1}(t)M}{\sqrt{D}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{K}\bigg(\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)-C_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)\Bigg);

(D.22)

\displaystyle C_{3}(t+1)=

\displaystyle C_{3}(t)\!-\!\eta\frac{C_{1}(t)M(1\!-\!Kp(t))}{\sqrt{D}(D-K)}\!\Bigg(\!\!\bigg(\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}\bigg)-C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)\!\Bigg).

(D.23)

In addition, based on all these definitions, the coefficients
C_{1}(t)
,
C_{2}(t)
, and
C_{3}(t)
are essentially minimizing the following loss function by gradient descent

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\frac{c_{\sigma}D\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{2(D-K)}\bigg[K(D-K)\bigg(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}p\bigg)^{2}+C_{1}^{2}\Big(1-Kp\Big)^{2}\bigg]-D\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}F_{6}(C_{1},p).

where
c_{\sigma}
is an absolute constant such that
c_{\sigma}=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is identity map}\}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is ReLU}\}}+\frac{1+\kappa^{2}}{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sigma(\cdot)\text{ is Leaky ReLU}\}}
. In addition,
F_{6}(C_{1},p)
is defined as

\displaystyle F_{6}=\begin{cases}0;&\text{ If }\sigma(\cdot)\text{is identity map}\\
pC_{1}^{2}\Bigg(Kp\bigg(\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan\bigg(\frac{p\sqrt{K(D-K)}}{1-Kp}\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg)+\frac{(1-Kp)\sqrt{K}}{\pi\sqrt{D-K}}\Bigg);&\text{ If }\sigma(\cdot)\text{is ReLU activation}\\
(1-\kappa)^{2}pC_{1}^{2}\Bigg(Kp\bigg(\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan\bigg(\frac{p\sqrt{K(D-K)}}{1-Kp}\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\bigg)+\frac{(1-Kp)\sqrt{K}}{\pi\sqrt{D-K}}\Bigg).&\text{ If }\sigma(\cdot)\text{is Leaky ReLU activation}\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
D.8
.

Similarly, it can be easily verified that the initialization
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(0)}=\mathbf{0}_{D\times D}
satisfies (
D.21
). Assuming it holds at the
t
-th iteration, we aim to prove that it continues to hold at the
t+1
-th iteration. To do this, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})=-c_{2}(t)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}+c_{3}(t)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top},

(D.24)

where
c_{2}(t)
and
c_{3}(t)
are two time-dependent non-positive scalars.
By Lemma
D.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{D}\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\!\!\sum_{m=1}^{M}\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\!-\!\sigma\bigg(\!\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg]\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\!\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\!\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}(\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\!-\!\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}})\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{3}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{4}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{5}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{6}}.

(D.25)

In the next, we discuss the value of
I_{3}
,
I_{4}
,
I_{5}
, and
I_{6}
respectively. For
I_{3}
, it can be calculated as

\displaystyle I_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i^{\prime},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)}{Kp(t)}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle p(t)+\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i},i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)\bigg)

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle p(t)+\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i},i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)+\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\bigg)\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle p(t)\Bigg]\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{1}(t)}{Kp(t)}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)\bigg)

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}+\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)+\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i},i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\bigg)\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle p(t)\Bigg]\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)M}{Kp(t)}F_{4}\bigg(p(t)^{2},(K-1)p(t)^{2},\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{D-K}\bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{1}(t)M}{Kp(t)}F_{5}\bigg(Kp(t)^{2},\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}},\frac{(D-K-1)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}}\bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)MF_{4}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}+\frac{C_{1}(t)MF_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

Notice that
\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle p(t)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,p(t)^{2})
,
\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i},i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,(K-1)p(t)^{2})
, and

\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{(1-Kp(t))^{2}}{D-K})
are three independent Gaussian random variables. Consequently, the first term in the penultimate equality is derived by the definition of
F_{4}(a,b,c)
in (
D.5
). Similarly,
\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,Kp(t)^{2})
,
\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{(1-Kp(t))^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}})
, and
\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i},i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{(D-K-1)(1-Kp(t))^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}})
are three independent Gaussian random variables. Therefore, the second term in the penultimate equality is derived by the definition of
F_{5}(a,b,c)
in (
D.6
). Similarly, for
I_{4}
, we can calculate it as

\displaystyle I_{4}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}\bigg)\Bigg]\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)}{Kp(t)}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)+\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\bigg)

\displaystyle\cdot\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)+\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\bigg)\Bigg]\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)M}{K}F_{3}\bigg(\!Kp(t)^{2}\!\!,\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{D-K}\!\bigg)\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\!\!\sum_{i_{2}\in G^{i}}\!\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\!\!

\displaystyle+\!\frac{C_{1}(t)M\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{(D\!-\!K)Kp(t)}F_{3}\bigg(\!Kp(t)^{2}\!\!,\frac{\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{D\!-\!K}\!\bigg)\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\!\!\sum_{i_{2}\notin G^{i}}\!\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)MF_{3}^{(t)}}{K}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}+\frac{C_{1}(t)M\big(1-Kp(t)\big)F_{3}^{(t)}}{(D-K)Kp(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

The penultimate equality holds by the definition of
F_{3}(a,b)
in (
D.4
), as
\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle p(t)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,Kp(t)^{2})
, and
\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{(1-Kp(t))^{2}}{D-K}\big)
are two independent Gaussian random variables.
Additionally,
I_{5}
can be calculated as

\displaystyle I_{5}

\displaystyle=C_{1}(t)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\mathbf{S}_{i^{\prime},i}^{(t)}\Bigg]\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{1}(t)^{2}\!\sum_{m=1}^{M}\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\!\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle p(t)\Bigg]\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle+C_{1}(t)^{2}\!\sum_{m=1}^{M}\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\!\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\Bigg]\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=MC_{1}(t)^{2}F_{2}\bigg(p(t)^{2},(K-1)p(t)^{2}+\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{D-K}\bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+MC_{1}(t)^{2}F_{2}\bigg(\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}},Kp(t)^{2}+\frac{(D-K-1)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}}\bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=MC_{1}(t)^{2}F_{2,1}^{(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}+MC_{1}(t)^{2}F_{2,2}^{(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top},

where the penultimate equality utilize the definition of
F_{2}(a,b)
in (
D.3
). Similarly, for
I_{6}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{6}

\displaystyle=C_{1}(t)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\Bigg]\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{S}_{i_{2},i}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=MC_{1}(t)^{2}p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}+MC_{1}(t)^{2}F_{1}^{(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{D-K}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}.

Combining all these results of
I_{3}
,
I_{4}
,
I_{5}
, and
I_{6}
, and plugging them into (
D.1
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{1}(t)M}{\sqrt{D}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{K}\bigg(\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)-C_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)\Bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}(t)M\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{(D-K)\sqrt{D}}\!\Bigg(\!\!\bigg(\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}\bigg)\!-\!C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)\!\Bigg)\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\!\!\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\!\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-c_{2}(t)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}+c_{3}(t)\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\mathbf{p}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}.

It remains to show that
c_{2}(t)
and
c_{3}(t)
are always non-negative. Notice that Lemma
D.5
guarantee the assumption of Lemma
D.7
. By carefully compare the formulas and applying Lemma
D.7
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{K\sqrt{D}c_{2}(t)}{MC_{1}(t)}\geq\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{5Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}A(t)\geq 0.

Since we have proved that
C_{1}(t)
is always non-negative in Lemma
D.4
, this result implies that
c_{2}(t)\geq 0
. Similarly, for
c_{3}(t)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{D}(D-K)c_{3}(t)}{MC_{1}(t)(1\!-\!Kp(t))}\geq\frac{1-Kp(t)}{5Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}A(t)\geq 0.

This proves that
c_{3}(t)\geq 0
, and we conclude that

\displaystyle C_{2}(t+1)=

\displaystyle C_{2}(t)+\eta\frac{C_{1}(t)M}{\sqrt{D}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{K}\bigg(\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)-C_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)\Bigg);

\displaystyle C_{3}(t+1)=

\displaystyle C_{3}(t)\!-\!\eta\frac{C_{1}(t)M(1\!-\!Kp(t))}{\sqrt{D}(D-K)}\!\Bigg(\!\!\bigg(\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}\bigg)-C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)\!\Bigg),

which completes the proof of (
D.21
), (
D.22
) and (
D.23
). It remains to prove the conclusions regarding
\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}
and
p(t)
. By the orthogonality among the positional encodings
\mathbf{p}_{i}
’s, it is straightforward that for all
i,i_{1}\in[D]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i}=\begin{cases}C_{2}(t)&\text{if }i_{1}\in G^{i};\\
-C_{3}(t)&\text{if }i_{1}\notin G^{i}.\end{cases}

Then by the definition of
\mathbf{S}^{(t)}
, when
i_{1}\in G^{i}

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\Big({\frac{\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i}}{\sqrt{D}}}\big)}{\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\exp\Big({\frac{\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}\mathbf{p}_{i}}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}=\frac{\exp\Big({\frac{C_{2}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}{K\exp\Big({\frac{C_{2}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}=p(t).

Since
C_{2}(t)
and
C_{3}(t)
are non-negative and monotonically increasing scalars, we immediately conclude that
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t)\leq\frac{1}{K}
, and
p(t)
is also monotonically increasing.
Lastly, it remains to formulate excess loss into an expression of excess loss. By the parameter forms in Lemma
D.2
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V},\mathbf{W}_{KQ})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{opt}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\Bigg(\sigma\bigg(\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle}{K}\bigg)-\sigma\bigg(C_{1}p\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle+\frac{C_{1}(1-Kp)}{D-K}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\bigg)\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle}{K}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]}_{I_{7}}+\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(C_{1}p\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle+\frac{C_{1}(1-Kp)}{D-K}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]}_{I_{8}}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle}{K}\bigg)\sigma\bigg(C_{1}p\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle+\frac{C_{1}(1-Kp)}{D-K}\sum_{i^{\prime}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle\bigg)\Bigg]}_{I_{9}}.

For the term
I_{7}
and
I_{8}
, by the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\sigma(x)^{2}]=c_{\sigma}a
when
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
, we can directly calculate that

\displaystyle I_{7}=\frac{c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K};

\displaystyle I_{8}=c_{\sigma}Kp^{2}C_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{\sigma}C_{1}^{2}(1-Kp)^{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{D-K}.

In addition, by utilizing the conclusions in Lemma
F.8
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle I_{9}=c_{\sigma}C_{1}p\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{D\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}F_{6}(C_{1},p)}{2}.

Plugging all these results, we completes the proof that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})=\frac{c_{\sigma}D\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{2(D-K)}\bigg[K(D-K)\bigg(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}p\bigg)^{2}+C_{1}^{2}\Big(1-Kp\Big)^{2}\bigg]-D\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}F_{6}(C_{1},p)

Now, we successfully prove all the conclusions of Lemma
D.8
.
∎

Lastly, before we prove Lemma
D.5
, we first introduce and prove the following Lemma
D.9
, Lemma
D.10
, Lemma
D.11
, and Lemma
D.12
, which will be utilized for proof of Lemma
D.5
.

Lemma D.9
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
3.1
and
p(t)
as defined in Lemma
D.2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{2\sqrt{D}}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big)\leq\Delta p(t)\leq\frac{D^{2}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D^{2}-1)}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big);

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\leq\eta\frac{MD}{K^{2}(D-K)}\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}\leq\frac{1}{D^{2}},

where
\Delta p(t)=p(t+1)-p(t)
,
\Delta C_{2}(t)=C_{2}(t+1)-C_{2}(t)
, and
\Delta C_{3}(t)=C_{3}(t+1)-C_{3}(t)
.

Proof of Lemma
D.9
.

By the definition of
p(t)
in Lemma
D.2
, it can be derived that

\displaystyle\Delta p(t)

\displaystyle=p(t+1)-p(t)=\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t+1)+C_{3}(t+1)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}-\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}\Big)}-\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big)(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}{\sqrt{D}\Big[K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}\Big)\Big]\Big[K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)\Big]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}-\Delta C_{2}(t)-\Delta C_{3}(t)}\frac{(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}{\Big[K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)\Big]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}-\Delta C_{2}(t)-\Delta C_{3}(t)}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)

Additionally, applying the update rules for
C_{2}(t)
and
C_{3}(t)
derived in Lemma
D.8
, along with a similar calculation to the one used in the proof of Lemma
D.7
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)\leq

\displaystyle\eta\frac{MC_{1}(t)}{K\sqrt{D}}\bigg(\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\frac{MC_{1}(t)}{K\sqrt{D}}\Bigg(\bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(t)}-1\bigg)A(t)+\bigg(\frac{(D-K)p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}-1\bigg)B(t)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\frac{MC_{1}(t)}{K\sqrt{D}}\bigg(\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)}A(t)+\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)+1-Kp(t)}B(t)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\frac{MC_{1}(t)}{K\sqrt{D}}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)}F_{3}^{(t)}\leq\eta\frac{MC_{1}(t)}{K\sqrt{D}}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)}\bigg(Kp(t)^{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D\!-\!K}}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\frac{M}{K^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{DK}}.

Here, the penultimate inequality holds as
C_{1}(t)\leq\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}
and
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t)\leq\frac{1}{K}
. Similarly, we can also derive that

\displaystyle\Delta C_{3}(t)\leq\eta\frac{MC_{1}(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D-K)}\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\leq\eta\frac{M}{K(D-K)}\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{DK}}.

Combining these results, we have

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\leq\eta\frac{MD}{K^{2}(D-K)}\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}\leq\frac{1}{D^{2}},

where the last inequality holds by the condition that
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}(M^{-1}D^{-5/2})
in Theorem
3.1
.
Replacing these results, we finally prove that

\displaystyle\Delta p(t)\leq\frac{D^{2}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D^{2}-1)}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big).

On the other hand, since
\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)
is sufficiently small, we can also have

\displaystyle\Delta p(t)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)}{2\sqrt{D}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)}{2\sqrt{D}}\frac{(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)}{\Big[K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-{\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}}\Big)\Big]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{2\sqrt{D}}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.10
.

For
C_{1}^{*}(t)
defined in Lemma
D.2
, it hols that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically increasing w.r.t
t
when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.10
.

As Lemma
D.8
demonstrates that
p(t)
is always monotonically increasing. Consequently, it suffices to show that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically increasing w.r.t.
p(t)
when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
. In the following, we discuss the three scenarios where
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, ReLU activation function, and Leaky ReLU activation function, respectively. When
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)=\frac{(D-K)p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}=\frac{D-K}{DKp(t)+\frac{1}{p(t)}-2K}.

It is straightforward that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically increasing when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{DK}}
, as the denominator is decreasing. When
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)=\frac{\pi(D-K)p(t)+2(D-K)p(t)\arctan\Big(\sqrt{K(D-K)}\frac{p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\Big)+2(D-K)\frac{1-Kp(t)}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}}{2\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)}.

By applying basic calculus, we can derive that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}C_{1}^{*}(t)}{\mathrm{d}p(t)}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi(\pi-1)(D-K)\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(D-K)\Big(\pi p(t)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}\Big)4\pi K\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi(D-K)\big(1-4\pi DKp(t)^{2}\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}}+\frac{3\pi(D-K)\big(1-2\sqrt{\pi DK}p(t)\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}},

which is positive when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
. Therefore, we can conclude that when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
,
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically increasing w.r.t.
p(t)
. Similarly, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, we also have,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)=

\displaystyle\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}\pi(D\!-\!K)p(t)+2(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)p(t)\arctan\Big(\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}\frac{p(t)}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\Big)}{2(1+\kappa^{2})\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)(1\!-\!Kp(t))}{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}(1+\kappa^{2})\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)},

and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}C_{1}^{*}(t)}{\mathrm{d}p(t)}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi(\pi-1)(D-K)\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(D-K)\Big(\pi p(t)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}\Big)K\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}{\pi\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi(D-K)\big(1-4\pi DKp(t)^{2}\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}}+\frac{3\pi(D-K)\big(1-2\sqrt{\pi DK}p(t)\big)}{4\pi^{2}\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1\big)^{2}},

which proves that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically increasing w.r.t.
p(t)
when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
.
∎

Lemma D.11
.

For
C_{1}^{*}(t)
defined in Lemma
D.2
, it holds that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{3DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}C_{1}^{*}(t),

(D.26)

Proof of Lemma
D.11
.

We prove (
D.26
) for
\sigma(\cdot)
is identity map, ReLU activation function, and Leaky ReLU activation function, respectively. When
\sigma(\cdot)
is identity map,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{(D-K)p(t+1)}{DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1}\geq\frac{(D-K)p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1+DK\Delta p(t)(2p(t)+\Delta p(t))}

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{DK\Delta p(t)(2p(t)+\Delta p(t))}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}C_{1}^{*}(t)\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{3DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}C_{1}^{*}(t),

where the second inequality holds by Lemma
F.9
, and the last inequality holds by
\Delta p(t)\leq p(t)
implied by Lemma
D.9
. When
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi(D-K)p(t+1)+2(D-K)p(t+1)\arctan\big(\sqrt{K(D-K)}\frac{p(t+1)}{1-Kp(t+1)}\big)}{2\pi(DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(D-K)(1-Kp(t+1))}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}\pi(DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\pi(D-K)p(t)+2(D-K)p(t)\arctan\big(\sqrt{K(D-K)}\frac{p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\big)+2(D-K)\frac{1-Kp(t)}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}}{2\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)+2\pi DK\Delta p(t)(2p(t)+\Delta p(t))}

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{3DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}C_{1}^{*}(t),

where the first inequality holds as the numerator is a monotonically increasing function w.r.t.
p(t)
. Furthermore, the second inequality holds by Lemma
F.9
, and
\Delta p(t)\leq p(t)
implied by Lemma
D.9
. Similarly, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}\pi(D\!-\!K)}{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}}p(t\!+\!1)+2(D\!-\!K)p(t\!+\!1)\arctan\big(\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}\frac{p(t+1)}{1\!-\!Kp(t+1)}\big)+2(D\!-\!K)\frac{1\!-\!Kp(t+1)}{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}}}{\frac{2\pi(1+\kappa^{2})}{(1-\kappa)^{2}}(DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}\pi(D\!-\!K)}{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}}p(t)+2(D-K)p(t)\arctan\big(\sqrt{K(D-K)}\frac{p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\big)+2(D-K)\frac{1-Kp(t)}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}}{\frac{2\pi(1+\kappa^{2})}{(1-\kappa)^{2}}(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)+\frac{2\pi(1+\kappa^{2})}{(1-\kappa)^{2}}DK\Delta p(t)(2p(t)+\Delta p(t))}

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{3DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}C_{1}^{*}(t).

This completes the proof
∎

Lemma D.12
.

For
A(t)
,
B(t)
defined in Lemma
D.5
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{A(t+1)}{A(t+1)+B(t+1)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)\big(Dp(t+1)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}-\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{2DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)^{2}}.

(D.27)

Proof of Lemma
D.12
.

Notice that
\frac{B(t)}{A(t)}
is a non-increasing function w.r.t.
p(t)
. Therefore, we can derive that

\displaystyle\frac{A(t+1)}{A(t+1)+B(t+1)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)\big(Dp(t+1)-1\big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A(t+1)}{A(t+1)+B(t+1)}\bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(t+1)\big(Dp(t+1)-1\big)}-\frac{1}{Dp(t+1)-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)+\Delta p(t)\big(2DKp(t)+DK\Delta p(t)-K\big)}-\frac{1}{Dp(t)-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}-\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{\Delta p(t)\big(2DKp(t)+DK\Delta p(t)-K\big)}{K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}-\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{2DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)^{2}},

where the last inequality holds
\delta p(t)\leq\frac{1}{D}
implied by Lemma
D.9
. This completes the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Lemma
D.5
.

Proof of Lemma
D.5
.

As Lemma
D.10
guarantees that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically increasing when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
. Consequently, when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-C_{1}(t+1)

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}^{*}(t)-C_{1}(t+1)=\bigg(1-\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)C_{1}^{*}(t)}\bigg)\Big(C_{1}^{*}(t)-C_{1}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1-\frac{\eta D}{K}\bigg)\Big(C_{1}^{*}(t)-C_{1}(t)\Big)\geq\bigg(1-\frac{\eta D}{K}\bigg)^{t+1}\Big(C_{1}^{*}(0)-C_{1}(0)\Big)\geq 0.

The second inequality holds by
\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\leq\frac{1}{K}
, and
C_{1}^{*}(t)\geq 1
. The last inequality holds by the assumption of
\eta
in Theorem
3.1
, and
C_{1}(0)=0
. In the next, we prove that (
D.17
) holds when
p(t)\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
by induction. We assume (
D.17
) holds at
t
-th iteration and examine the
t+1
-th iteration. Inspired by the separating strategy in
56
, we consider the following two cases: (i). when
C_{1}(t)\leq\big(1+\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
and (ii). when
\big(1+\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq C_{1}(t)\leq\big(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
. For the first case, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t).

(D.28)

This is because if
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)-C_{1}(t+1)

\displaystyle=\bigg(1-\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)C_{1}^{*}(t)}\bigg)\Big(C_{1}^{*}(t)-C_{1}(t)\Big)\geq 0,

which implies that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1).

The last inequality is guaranteed by (
D.1
). On the other hand, if
C_{1}^{*}(t)<C_{1}(t)\leq\big(1+\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
, then we can also obtain that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(t)\leq\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1).

In the next, we show that (
D.1
) holds. By applying the lower bounds derived in Lemma
D.11
and Lemma
D.12
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle-\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)\frac{3DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1}C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle-\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{2DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)^{2}}C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{2\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{15Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle-\frac{3(4\pi+1)DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)+1-Kp(t)}C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{10\pi DKp(t)\Delta p(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg(\frac{2}{15}-\frac{(22\pi+3)Kp(t)^{2}}{D}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t),

which finishes the proof of (
D.1
). In the derivation above, the second inequality holds as
\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\geq\frac{1}{3}
and
\frac{1}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\leq 5\pi
when
p(t)\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
. The penultimate inequality is derived by Lemma
D.9
. As we demonstrated previously, (
D.1
) implies that (
D.17
) holds at the
t+1
-th iteration for the first case. In the following, we consider the second case, where
\big(1+\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq C_{1}(t)\leq\big(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
. For this case, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{\eta D\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{10K\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1)

(D.29)

This is because

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)=

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)+\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\bigg(1-\frac{C_{1}(t)}{C_{1}^{*}(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{\eta D\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{10K\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1),

where the penultimate inequality is derived by
F_{3}^{(t)}=A(t)+B(t)
and
A(t)\geq\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{4}
, and the last inequality is guaranteed by (
D.1
). To show (
D.1
) holds, by applying Lemma
D.7
, we derive an refined upper bound for
\Delta C_{2}(t)
and
\Delta C_{3}(t)
as follows:

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta MC_{1}(t)}{K\sqrt{D}}\bigg(\frac{F_{4}^{(t)}}{p(t)}-F_{3}^{(t)}-KC_{1}(t)\Big(F_{2,1}^{(t)}+p(t)F_{1}^{(t)}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\eta MC_{1}(t)}{5K\sqrt{D}}\frac{\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}A(t)\leq\frac{3(4\pi+1)\eta Mp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{5K\sqrt{D}\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t),

and

\displaystyle\Delta C_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta MC_{1}(t)\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D-K)}\Bigg(\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\!-\!\frac{(D-K)F_{5}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}-C_{1}(t)\bigg(F_{1}^{(t)}-\frac{(D\!-\!K)F_{2,2}^{(t)}}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\eta MC_{1}(t)\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{5\sqrt{D}(D-K)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}A(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3(4\pi+1)\eta Mp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{5(D-K)\sqrt{D}\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t).

Based on these refined upper bounds for
\Delta C_{2}(t),\Delta C_{3}(t)
, and the lower bounds obtained previously, we can derive that

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t\!+\!1)\!+\!B(t\!+\!1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t\!+\!1)(Dp(t\!+\!1)\!-\!1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t\!+\!1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)-\bigg(1\!+\!\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{(22\pi+3)D\Delta p(t)}{Dp(t)-1}C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(22\pi+3)D}{Dp(t)-1}\frac{D^{2}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D^{2}-1)}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3(22\pi+3)(4\pi+1)}{5}\frac{\eta D\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{K\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\frac{D^{3}Mp(t)^{2}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}}{(D^{2}-1)D(D-K)\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta D\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{K\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}\frac{60\pi M}{DK^{2}}\leq\frac{\eta D\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{10K\big(Dp(t)\!-\!1\big)}.

Here, the first inequality is derived by applying the upper bound of
\big(1+\frac{4A(t+1)}{5(A(t+1)+B(t+1))}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t+1)
obtained previously. The second inequality holds by applying Lemma
D.9
. The third inequality is derived by replacing the refined upper bound of
\Delta C_{2}(t)
and
\Delta C_{3}(t)
. The penultimate inequality holds as
C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq\frac{1}{Kp(t)}
, and the last inequality is guaranteed by
D\geq\Omega(M)
in the condition of Theorem
3.1
. This demonstrates that (
D.1
) holds in the second case, which completes the proof of (
D.17
).
∎

D.2
Three phases training

In the previous section, Lemma
D.2
accurately characterizes the training dynamics of
\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)}
and
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}
. Specifically, it demonstrates that
\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)}=C_{1}(t)\mathbf{V}^{*}
, where
C_{1}(t)
is always upper bounded by
\big(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
. Next, we will show that the update pattern of
C_{1}(t)
differs across three distinct phases. In the first phase,
C_{1}(t)
monotonically increases, approaching
C_{1}^{*}(t)
while
p(t)
remains close to
\frac{1}{D}
.
In the second phase,
C_{1}(t)
remains in a neighborhood of
C_{1}^{*}(t)
, while
p(t)
monotonically increases. This increase exhibits modes characteristic of a tensor power progression, continuing until
p(t)
reaches
\frac{1}{2K}
. In the third phase, the
p(t)
will eventually converges to
\frac{1}{K}
, and
C_{1}^{*}(t)
converges to 1, leading the loss also to converge. The formal proof is provided as follows.

Lemma D.13
.

Under the same conditions with Theorem
3.1
, there exist
t_{1}=\Theta(\eta^{-1})
, such that
C_{1}(t_{1})\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t_{1})
, and
p(t)\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
for all
t\leq t_{1}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.13
.

Notice that when
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
,
C_{1}(t)
is monotonically increasing. Let
t_{1}
be the first time such that
C_{1}(t)\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
. For the conclusion regarding
p(t)
with
t\leq t_{1}
, we first assume it holds and utilize it to demonstrate other conclusions, and lastly prove it by induction. Since
p(t)
almost remain unchanged for all
t\leq t_{1}
, we can obtain that
0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})
for all
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\leq t_{1}
(This conclusion is proved in following Lemma
D.14
). Therefore for all
t<t_{1}

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)-C_{1}(t)=\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\bigg(1-\frac{C_{1}(t)}{C_{1}^{*}(t)}\bigg)\geq\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}\bigg(1-\frac{C_{1}(t_{1}-1)}{\frac{0.95}{0.975}C_{1}^{*}(t_{1}-1)}\bigg)\geq\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{40Kp(t)},

where the last inequality holds by
\frac{C_{1}(t_{1}-1)}{C_{1}^{*}(t_{1}-1)}\leq 0.95
. On the other hand, it is straightforward that
C(t+1)-C(t)\leq\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}
. Additionally, when
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t),p(t_{1})\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
, we can obtain that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}=p(t)=\Theta\bigg(\frac{1}{D}\bigg);

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t_{1})=\frac{F_{3}^{(t_{1})}}{Kp(t_{1})F_{1}^{(t_{1})}}=\frac{\Theta(1)}{Kp(t_{1})(Dp(t_{1})-1)+1-Kp(t_{1})}=\Theta(1).

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}=\frac{p(t)}{4}+\frac{p(t)}{2\pi}\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\Big)+\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{K(D-K)}}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)=\Theta\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{DK}}\bigg);

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t_{1})=\frac{F_{3}^{(t_{1})}}{Kp(t_{1})F_{1}^{(t_{1})}}=\frac{\Theta\big(\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\big)}{Kp(t_{1})(Dp(t_{1})-1)+1-Kp(t_{1})}=\Theta\Big(\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\Big).

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\frac{F_{3}^{(t)}}{Kp(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}p(t)}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}p(t)}{2\pi}\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D-K)}p(t)}{1-Kp(t)}\Big)+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{2\pi\sqrt{K(D-K)}}\big(1-Kp(t)\big)

\displaystyle=\Theta\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{DK}}\bigg);

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{F_{3}^{(t_{1})}}{Kp(t_{1})F_{1}^{(t_{1})}}=\frac{\Theta\big(\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\big)}{Kp(t_{1})(Dp(t_{1})-1)+1-Kp(t_{1})}=\Theta\Big(\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\Big).

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle t_{1}=\frac{0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t_{1})}{\frac{1}{t_{1}}\sum_{t=0}^{t_{1}-1}\Delta C_{1}(t)}=\begin{cases}\frac{\Theta(1)}{\Theta(\eta)}=\Theta(\eta^{-1}),&\ \text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{is identity map};\\
\frac{\Theta\big(\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\big)}{\Theta\big(\eta\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\big)}=\Theta(\eta^{-1}),&\ \text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{is ReLU activation function};\\
\frac{\Theta\big(\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\big)}{\Theta\big(\eta\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}\big)}=\Theta(\eta^{-1}),&\ \text{if }\sigma(\cdot)\text{is Leaky ReLU activation function}.\end{cases}

Next we prove that
p(t_{1})\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
by induction. Assume it holds at
t
-th iteration, then by Lemma
D.9
we can derive that

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\leq\eta\frac{MD}{K^{2}(D-K)}\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{DK}},

and consequently

\displaystyle\Delta p(t)\leq\frac{D^{2}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D^{2}-1)}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big)\leq\frac{3M\eta}{\sqrt{K^{5}D^{3}}}.

Therefore, we can eventually conclude that

\displaystyle p(t_{1})\leq p(0)+\sum_{t=0}^{t_{1}-1}\Delta p(t)\leq\frac{1}{D}+\Theta\bigg(\frac{M}{\sqrt{K^{5}D^{3}}}\bigg)\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D},

where the last inequality is derived by our condition that
D=\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(M)\big)
in Theorem
3.1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.14
.

For all
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\leq t_{1}
, where
t_{1}
is defined in Lemma
D.13
, it holds that
0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})
.

Proof of Lemma
D.14
.

Notice that by the definition of
C_{1}^{*}(t)
, it is entirely determined by
p(t)
. And for all
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\leq t_{1}
, we all have
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t^{\prime}),p(t^{\prime\prime})\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
. With Lemma
F.1
and Lemma
F.5
, we can further derive that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{(D-K)p(t^{\prime})}{DKp(t^{\prime})^{2}-2Kp(t^{\prime})+1}\leq\frac{D-K}{\frac{D}{1+D^{-1/4}}-K(1-D^{-1/4})}\leq 1+D^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{(D-K)p(t^{\prime})}{DKp(t^{\prime})^{2}-2Kp(t^{\prime})+1}\geq\frac{D-K}{D-K}=1.

It immediately concludes that

\displaystyle 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})-0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})\geq\frac{1}{40}-D^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq 0,

as
D\geq\Omega(1)
.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(D-K)F_{3}^{(t^{\prime})}}{Kp(t^{\prime})\big(DKp(t^{\prime})^{2}-2Kp(t^{\prime})+1\big)}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+\frac{(D-K)p(t^{\prime})}{2}}{1-Kp(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+\frac{(D-K)p(t^{\prime})}{2}+\frac{Kp(t^{\prime})\big(\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+\frac{(D-K)p(t^{\prime})}{2}\big)}{\big(1-Kp(t^{\prime})\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+1+\frac{K}{D}+\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D}}\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+2,

where the penultimate and last inequalities hold by utilizing Lemma
F.10
, and
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t^{\prime})\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
,
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(K)\big)
in the conditions of Theorem
3.1
. Similarly, we can also obtain that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(D-K)F_{3}^{(t^{\prime\prime})}}{Kp(t^{\prime\prime})\big(DKp(t^{\prime\prime})^{2}-2Kp(t^{\prime\prime})+1\big)}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}}{1+DKp(t^{\prime\prime})^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}-\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}DKp(t^{\prime\prime})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}-1,

where the penultimate and last inequalities hold by utilizing Lemma
F.9
, and
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t^{\prime})\leq\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
,
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(K)\big)
in the conditions of Theorem
3.1
. Based on these two results, it is straightforward that

\displaystyle 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})-0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})\geq\frac{1}{40\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}-3\geq 0,

as
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(K)\big)
.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(D-K)F_{3}^{(t^{\prime})}}{(1+\kappa^{2})Kp(t^{\prime})\big(DKp(t^{\prime})^{2}-2Kp(t^{\prime})+1\big)}\leq\frac{\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})(D-K)p(t^{\prime})}{2}}{(1+\kappa^{2})\big(1-Kp(t^{\prime})\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{(1+\kappa^{2})\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}+2;

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(D-K)F_{3}^{(t^{\prime})}}{(1+\kappa^{2})Kp(t^{\prime})\big(DKp(t^{\prime})^{2}-2Kp(t^{\prime})+1\big)}\geq\frac{\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}}{(1+\kappa)^{2}\big(1+DKp(t^{\prime\prime})^{2}\big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{(1+\kappa^{2})\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}-1.

Combining these results directly leads to

\displaystyle 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime\prime})-0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t^{\prime})\geq\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}}{40(1+\kappa^{2})\pi}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}-3\geq 0,

as
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(K)\big)
.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
D.13
successfully demonstrate that at the initial phase of training,
C_{1}(t)
will monotonically increases until
0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
, while
p(t)
remains smaller that
\frac{1+D^{-1/4}}{D}
. Furthermore, once
C_{1}(t)
reaches
0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
, it never falls below this threshold again.
Combined with the conclusion demonstrated in Lemma
D.5
that
C_{1}(t)
is always upper bounded by
\big(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
, we can claim that
C_{1}(t)
will always remain inner a neighborhood around
C_{1}^{*}(t)
. The following lemma provides a formal illustration.

Lemma D.15
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
3.1
and with
t_{1}
as defined in Lemma
D.13
, for all
t\geq t_{1}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)\geq\Bigg[0.95\vee\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)\Bigg]C_{1}^{*}(t),

(D.30)

where
A(t)
and
B(t)
are defined same as in Lemma
D.5
.

Before we prove Lemma
D.15
, we first introduce the following lemma, which will be utilized in the proof of Lemma
D.15
.

Lemma D.16
.

For
C_{1}^{*}(t)
defined in Lemma
D.2
, it always holds that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{3\big(D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)\big)}{2\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)\big)}\Delta p(t).

(D.31)

In addition,
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically decreasing when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.16
.

We prove this lemma by considering
\sigma(\cdot)
as the identity map, ReLU activation function, and Leaky ReLU activation function, respectively.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)=

\displaystyle\frac{(D-K)p(t+1)}{DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1}\leq\frac{(D-K)p(t)+(D-K)\Delta p(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-2K\Delta p(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)}\Delta p(t).

In addition,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)=\frac{D-K}{DKp(t)+\frac{1}{p(t)}-2K},

which is obviously decreasing when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi(D\!-\!K)p(t\!+\!1)+2(D\!-\!K)p(t\!+\!1)\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}p(t+1)}{1\!-\!Kp(t+1)}\Big)+2(D\!-\!K)\frac{1\!-\!Kp(t+1)}{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}}}{2\pi(DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi(D\!-\!K)p(t)\!+\!2(D\!-\!K)p(t)\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}p(t)}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\Big)}{2\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)-2\pi K\Delta p(t)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(D\!-\!K)\frac{1-Kp(t)}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}\!+\!3\pi(D\!-\!K)\Delta p(t)}{2\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)-2\pi K\Delta p(t)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{3\big(D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)\big)}{2\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)\big)}\Delta p(t).

In addition,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)=\frac{D-K}{2\big(DKp(t)\!+\!\frac{1}{p(t)}\!-\!2K\big)}+\frac{(D\!-\!K)\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}p(t)}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\Big)}{\pi\big(DKp(t)\!+\!\frac{1}{p(t)}\!-\!2K\big)}+\frac{\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{\pi\big(DKp(t)\!+\!\frac{1}{p(t)}\!-\!2K\big)},

where all these three terms are monotonically decreasing w.r.t.
p(t)
, when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
. This demonstrates that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically decreasing when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, then by a similar calculation process,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)p(t\!+\!1)+2(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)p(t\!+\!1)\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}p(t+1)}{1\!-\!Kp(t+1)}\Big)}{2(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\pi(DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)(1\!-\!Kp(t+1))}{(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\pi(DKp(t+1)^{2}-2Kp(t+1)+1)\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)p(t)\!+\!2(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)p(t)\arctan\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}p(t)}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\Big)}{2(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)-2\pi(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})K\Delta p(t)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)\frac{1-Kp(t)}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}+3\pi(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)\Delta p(t)}{2(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\pi(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1)-2\pi(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})K\Delta p(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{3\big(D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)\big)}{2\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)\big)}\Delta p(t).

In addition,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{*}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}(D-K)}{2(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\big(DKp(t)\!+\!\frac{1}{p(t)}\!-\!2K\big)}\!+\!\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}(D\!-\!K)\arctan\Big(\!\frac{\sqrt{K(D\!-\!K)}p(t)}{1\!-\!Kp(t)}\!\Big)}{\pi(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\big(DKp(t)\!+\!\frac{1}{p(t)}\!-\!2K\big)}

\displaystyle\quad\!+\!\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}\sqrt{\frac{D-K}{K}}\big(1\!-\!Kp(t)\big)}{\pi(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})\big(DKp(t)\!+\!\frac{1}{p(t)}\!-\!2K\big)},

where all these three terms are monotonically decreasing w.r.t.
p(t)
, when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
. This demonstrates that
C_{1}^{*}(t)
is monotonically decreasing when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
.

This completes the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Lemma
D.15

Proof of Lemma
D.15
.

We first prove the first part of (
D.30
), i.e.
C_{1}(t)\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
for all
t>t_{1}
, by induction.
To establish the conclusion, we consider two cases at the
t
-th iteration: (i). when
C_{1}(t)\geq 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
. (ii). when
0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq C_{1}(t)<0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
. For the first case, when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
, Lemma
D.5
shows that
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)
, implying that
C_{1}(t+1)\geq C_{1}(t)
. Then we can derive that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)\geq 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)\geq 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-\frac{3\big(D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)\big)}{2\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)\big)}\Delta p(t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-\frac{3(D-K)}{D^{2}}\geq 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-0.025\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1),

where the third inequality holds by applying the lower bound of
C_{1}^{*}(t)
demonstrated in Lemma
D.16
. The forth inequality holds as
C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}
, and
\Delta p(t)\leq\frac{1}{D^{2}}
guaranteed by Lemma
D.9
. The penultimate inequality holds as
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(K)\big)
, and the last inequality holds as
C_{1}^{*}(t)\geq 1
. When
p(t)\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
, the upper bound of
C_{1}(t)
established in Lemma
D.5
can help to derive that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)-\frac{4\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-\frac{3(D-K)}{D^{2}}-\frac{4\eta DA(t)}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-\frac{3(D-K)}{D^{2}}-\eta\sqrt{\frac{D}{K^{3}}}\geq 0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)-0.025\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1).

Here, the second inequality applies the previously obtained lower bound for
C_{1}(t)
. The third inequality holds as
A(t)\leq Kp(t)^{2}
, and
p(t)\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
. The penultimate inequality is derived by
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(K)\big)
and
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}(MD^{-5/2})
in the condition of Theorem
3.1
. These results demonstrate that under the first case,
C_{1}(t+1)\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t+1)
. Let’s consider the second case, where
0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq C_{1}(t)<0.975\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
. Under this case, it is obvious that
C_{1}(t+1)
would be larger than
C_{1}(t)
, and by the updating rule, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)+\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{40Kp(t)}\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{\eta DF_{3}^{(t)}}{40Kp(t)}-\frac{3\big(D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)\big)}{2\big(DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)\big)}\Delta p(t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)+\eta p(t)\bigg(\frac{D}{80}-\frac{3MD^{3}(1-Kp(t))}{\sqrt{D}(D^{2}-1)(D-K)K^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{D+1}{K}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)+\eta p(t)\bigg(\frac{D}{80}-\frac{4M}{K^{\frac{5}{2}}}\bigg)\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t).

Here, the second inequality holds by (
D.31
), the third inequality holds since
F_{3}^{(t)}\geq\frac{Kp(t)^{2}}{2}
by Lemma
F.5
,
\frac{D-K+KC_{1}^{*}(t)}{DKp(t)^{2}-2Kp(t)+1-K\Delta p(t)}\leq 2D
, and applying the conclusion of upper bound of
\Delta p(t)
demonstrated in Lemma
D.9
. Besides, the last two inequalities is guaranteed by
D\geq\Omega\big(\mathrm{poly}(M,K)\big)
. This finishes the proof of
C_{1}(t)\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
for all
t\geq t_{1}
. In the next, we prove the second part of (
D.30
), i.e.
C_{1}(t)\geq\big(1-\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
. In fact, we only need to consider the scenario where
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
. This is because when
p(t)\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4(1-Kp(t))}{5\Big(Kp(t)+\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{K}{D-K}}(1-Kp(t))\Big)(Dp(t)-1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{10\sqrt{DK}p(t)}\geq 0.05.

Therefore,
C_{1}(t)\geq 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
guarantee that
C_{1}(t)\geq\big(1-\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
holds when
p(t)\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
. When
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
, we also consider two cases: (i). when
C_{1}(t)>\big(1-\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
. (ii). when
\big(1-\frac{4A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq C_{1}(t)\leq\big(1-\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\big)C_{1}^{*}(t)
. Then, for the first case, at the
t+1
-th iteration, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)-\frac{4\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)+B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{4\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle+\frac{2A(t)}{5(A(t)\!+\!B(t))}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)\!-\!1)}C_{1}^{*}(t)-\frac{4\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle-\frac{A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\frac{\Delta p(t)\big(2DKp(t)\!+\!DK\Delta p(t)\!-\!K\big)}{K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)^{2}}C_{1}^{*}{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle+\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg(\frac{C_{1}^{*}(t)}{5}-\eta\frac{4D}{5}-\frac{DC_{1}^{*}(t)\Delta p(t)}{Dp(t)-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle+\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg(\frac{1}{5}-\frac{4}{5\sqrt{D^{3}}}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{D^{3}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1).

In particular, the third inequality is obtained by replacing the the lower bound of
\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}
in Lemma
D.12
, and utilizing
C_{1}^{*}(t)\geq C_{1}^{*}(t+1)
when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
, which is demonstrated in Lemma
D.16
. The forth inequality is derived by the facts
\frac{A(t)}{A(t)+B(t)}\geq\frac{1}{2}
when
p(t)\geq\frac{2}{\sqrt{DK}}
,
A(t)\leq Kp(t)^{2}
, and utilizing the upper bound of
\Delta p(t)
in Lemma
D.9
. Lastly, the penultimate inequality is derived as
1\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq\sqrt{\frac{D}{K}}
,
\Delta p(t)\leq\frac{1}{D^{5/2}}
, and
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}(D^{-5/2})
. This demonstrates that the second part of (
D.30
) holds at
t+1
-th iteration for the first case. On the other hand, for the second case,
C_{1}(t+1)
would be strictly larger than
C_{1}(t)
, and it can be demonstrated that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)+\frac{2\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t)}{5\big(A(t)+B(t)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)(Dp(t)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)+\frac{2\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\eta DA(t)(1-Kp(t))}{5K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}-\frac{A(t)}{A(t)\!+\!B(t)}\frac{\Delta p(t)\big(2DKp(t)\!+\!DK\Delta p(t)\!-\!K\big)}{K^{2}p(t)^{2}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)^{2}}C_{1}^{*}{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1)

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg(\frac{D}{5}-\frac{2DM\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{K^{7}}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1),

where the last inequality holds as
\frac{2DM\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{K^{7}}\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\leq\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{D}M}{K^{3}}\Big)\leq\mathcal{O}(D)
. This demonstrates that under the second case, we still have

\displaystyle C_{1}(t+1)\geq\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t+1)}{5\big(A(t+1)+B(t+1)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t+1)}{Kp(t+1)(Dp(t+1)-1)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t+1),

which finishes the proof of (
D.30
).
∎

Lemmas
D.15
and
D.5
together establish matching lower and upper bounds for
C_{1}(t)
after
t_{1}
. Based on these bounds, we can derive a precise training time at which
p(t)
achieves
\frac{1}{2K}
. This result is formally presented in the following lemma.

Lemma D.17
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
3.1
, there exists
T^{*}=\Theta\big(\frac{KD^{2}}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}\big)
, such that
p(T^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.17
.

Notice that Lemma
D.15
and Lemma
D.5
guarantee that

\displaystyle 0.95\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq C_{1}(t)\leq(4\pi+1)\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)

for all
t\geq t_{1}
. The left hand side inequality is straightforward, and the right hand side holds because: when
p(t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
,
C_{1}(t)\leq C_{1}^{*}(t)<(4\pi+1)\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t)
; when
p(t)\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi DK}}
,

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)\leq\bigg(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5\big(A(t)+B(t)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)\leq(4\pi+1)\cdot C_{1}^{*}(t).

On the other hand, Lemma
D.15
and Lemma
D.5
also guarantee that

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)\leq\bigg(1+\frac{4A(t)}{5\big(A(t)+B(t)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t);

\displaystyle C_{1}(t)\geq\bigg(1-\frac{4A(t)}{5\big(A(t)+B(t)\big)}\frac{1-Kp(t)}{Kp(t)\big(Dp(t)-1\big)}\bigg)C_{1}^{*}(t)

(D.32)

These two lower and upper bounds of
C_{1}(t)
allow us to apply Lemma
D.7
to derive lower and upper bounds for
\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)
as

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\frac{9(4\pi+1)Dp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{10K(D-K)\sqrt{D}\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t);

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)

\displaystyle\geq\eta\frac{19Dp(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{200K(D-K)\sqrt{D}\big(DKp(t)^{2}\!-\!2Kp(t)\!+\!1\big)}C_{1}^{*}(t),

(D.33)

where we replacing
M
with
\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
to match the presentation in our Theorem
3.1
.
With these bounds in hand, we denote
T^{*}
as the first time such that
p(t)\geq\frac{1}{2K}
. Then for all
t_{1}\leq t\leq T^{*}
,
by applying Lemma
D.9
and the upper and lower bounds of
\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)
obtained in (
D.2
), it can be derived that

\displaystyle\Delta p(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D^{2}p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{\sqrt{D}(D^{2}-1)}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big)\leq\eta\frac{(8\pi+2)\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K\sqrt{DK}}p(t)^{2};

\displaystyle\Delta p(t)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{p(t)\big(1-Kp(t)\big)}{2\sqrt{D}}\big(\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)\big)\geq\eta\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{50K\sqrt{DK}}p(t)^{2}.

Notice that the iterative rules for
p(t)
satisfying the assumptions in Lemma
F.11
. By applying Lemma
F.11
with the initialization that
\frac{1}{D}\leq p(t_{1})\leq\frac{2}{D}
, we can obtained that

\displaystyle T^{*}-t_{1}\leq\frac{50D^{2}K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}+100(8\pi+2)\big(\log D-\log K\big)\leq\Theta\Bigg(\frac{D^{2}K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle T^{*}-t_{1}\geq\frac{D^{2}K}{35\pi\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}-\big(\log D-\log K\big)\geq\Theta\Bigg(\frac{D^{2}K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg).

This results demonstrates that
T^{*}=t_{1}+\Theta\big(\frac{D^{2}K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}\big)=\Theta\big(\frac{D^{2}K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}\big)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

In the next, we provide the analysis for the last stage that
p(t)
