eventually converges to
\frac{1}{K}
. This result is formally presented in the following lemma.

Lemma D.18
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
3.1
, for any
T\geq T^{*}
, where
T^{*}=\Theta\big(\frac{D^{2}K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}\big)
as defined in Lemma
D.17
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}-\frac{20D(D-K)}{\sqrt{\eta K\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}(T-T^{*})}\leq p(T)\leq\frac{1}{K}-\frac{D(D-K)}{2e\sqrt{\eta K\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}(T-T^{*})}.

(D.34)

In addition, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|p(T)C_{1}(T)-\frac{1}{K}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{D}\big(1-Kp(T)\big)+\frac{1}{K}\big(1-Kp(T)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{40K(D-K)}{\sqrt{\eta K\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}(T-T^{*})}+\frac{400D^{2}(D-K)^{2}}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}(T-T^{*})}

(D.35)

Proof of Lemma
D.18
.

With the bounds established in Lemma
D.17
and the fact that
\frac{1-Kp(t)}{p(t)}=\exp\big(-\frac{C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)}{\sqrt{D}}\big)
, the upper and lower bounds of
\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)
obtained in (
D.2
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\frac{8\pi\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K^{3}\sqrt{D^{3}}}e^{-\frac{2}{\sqrt{D}}\big(C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)\big)};

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)

\displaystyle\geq\eta\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{200K^{3}\sqrt{D^{3}}}e^{-\frac{2}{\sqrt{D}}\big(C_{2}(t)+C_{3}(t)\big)}.

The upper and lower bounds of
\Delta C_{2}(t)+\Delta C_{3}(t)
match the assumptions of Lemma
F.12
. By applying the lemma, we can obtain that for all
T\geq T^{*}
,

\displaystyle C_{2}(T)+C_{3}(T)\geq\frac{\sqrt{D}}{2}\log\bigg(\frac{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{200K^{3}D^{2}}(T-T^{*})+e^{\frac{2}{K\sqrt{D}}}\bigg);

\displaystyle C_{2}(T)+C_{3}(T)\leq\eta\frac{8\pi\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K^{3}\sqrt{D^{3}}}+\frac{\sqrt{D}}{2}\log\bigg(\frac{8\pi\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K^{3}D^{2}}(T-T^{*})+e^{\frac{2}{K\sqrt{D}}}\bigg).

Replacing this result into the formula of
p(T)
, we have

\displaystyle p(T)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-\frac{C_{2}(T)+C_{3}(T)}{\sqrt{D}}\Big)}\geq\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\big(-\frac{1}{2}\log\big(\frac{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{200K^{3}D^{2}}(T-T^{*})\big)\big)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K}-\frac{20D(D-K)}{\sqrt{\eta K\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}(T-T^{*})}}

On the other hand, we can also derive that

\displaystyle p(T^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K+(D-K)\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\log\big(\frac{8\pi\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K^{3}D^{2}}(T-T^{*})+e^{\frac{2}{K\sqrt{D}}}\big)-\frac{\eta 4\pi\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K^{3}D^{3}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K}-\frac{D(D-K)}{2e\sqrt{\eta K\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}(T-T^{*})}}.

This finishes the proof of (
D.34
). With this condition holds, by checking the definition of
C_{1}^{*}(T^{*})
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle 1+\big(1-Kp(T)\big)\bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(T)}-\frac{1-Kp(T)}{(D-K)Kp(T)^{2}}\bigg)\leq C_{1}^{*}(T)\leq 1+\frac{1-Kp(T)}{Kp(T)}.

Plugging this result into (
D.2
), we derive that

\displaystyle 1+\big(1-Kp(T)\big)\bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(T)}-\frac{2K}{D}\bigg)\leq C_{1}(T)\leq 1+\big(1-Kp(T)\big)\bigg(\frac{1}{Kp(T)}+\frac{2K}{D}\bigg),

which immediately leads to the final conclusion of (
D.18
).

∎

Now, we are ready to prove Theorem
3.1
.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

We first prove the first conclusion.

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbf{S}^{(T)}-\mathbf{S}^{*}\Big\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\Big(\mathbf{S}_{i_{1},i}^{(T)}-\mathbf{S}^{*}_{i_{1},i}\Big)^{2}}=\sqrt{DK\bigg(\frac{1}{K}-p(T)\bigg)^{2}+D(D-K)\frac{\big(1-Kp(T)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{D}{\sqrt{K(D-K)}}\big(1-Kp(T)\big)=\Theta\Bigg(\frac{D^{\frac{5}{2}}}{\sqrt{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}(T-T^{*})}\Bigg),

where the last inequality holds by applying the upper and lower bounds of
p(T)
derived in Lemma
D.18
. This finishes the first conclusion of Theorem
3.1
. Notice that in Lemma
D.18
, we have derived that
|C_{1}(T)-1|=\Theta(1-Kp(T))
, which directly imply that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)}-\mathbf{V}^{*}\big\|_{F}=|C_{1}(T)-1|\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{F}=\Theta\Bigg(D^{2}\sqrt{\frac{K}{\eta\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}(T-T^{*})}\Bigg)\cdot\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\|_{F},

where the last inequality holds by applying the upper and lower bounds of
p(T)
derived in Lemma
D.18
. This finishes the second conclusion of Theorem
3.1
. For the third conclusion, notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\mathbf{Y}_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(T)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(T)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(T)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(T)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathcal{E}\|_{F}^{2}\big],

where the last term is essential
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}
, and the last inequality holds by the independence between
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathcal{E}
and the fact that
\mathcal{E}
is zero-mean. Since this equation holds, in the next, we directly deal with
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}
. We first prove the upper bound. By utilizing the fact that
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|\leq|x-y|
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(T)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(T)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\underbrace{\Big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\Big)\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}_{Z_{1,i,m}^{(T)}}+\underbrace{\frac{C_{1}(T)\big(1-Kp(T)\big)}{D-K}\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}_{Z_{2,i,m}^{(T)}}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg].

Notice that
Z_{1,i,m}^{(T)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{1,m}^{2})
, where
\sigma_{1,m}^{2}=K\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\big)^{2}
, and
Z_{2,i,m}^{(T)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{2,m}^{2})
, where
\sigma_{2,m}^{2}=\frac{\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}C_{1}(T)^{2}(1-Kp(T))^{2}}{D-K}
, and they are independent. Based on the upper bounds derived in Lemma
D.18
, we can finally derive that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(Z_{1,i,m}^{(T)}+Z_{2,i,m}^{(T)}\Big)^{2}\bigg]=\frac{D}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sigma_{1,m}^{2}+\frac{D}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sigma_{2,m}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{DK}{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\bigg)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{DC_{1}(T)^{2}\big(1-Kp(T)\big)^{2}}{2(D-K)}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{c}\frac{KD^{4}}{\eta(T-T^{*})}.

where the last inequality holds by applying the upper bounds for
\big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\big)^{2}
, and
p(T)
derived in Lemma
D.18

This completes the proof for upper bound. On the other hand, denote
Z_{3,m,i}=\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,K\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2})
and
Z_{4,m,i}=\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,(D-K)\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}\big)
, and
Z_{5,m,i}^{(T)}=p(T)Z_{3,m,i}+\frac{1-Kp(T)}{D-K}Z_{4,m,i}
. Then, by utilizing the fact that
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|\geq|x-y|\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0,y\geq 0\}}
, we can further derive that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(T)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(T)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sigma\bigg(\frac{Z_{3,m,i}}{K}\bigg)-\sigma\Big(C_{1}(T)Z_{5,m,i}^{(T)}\Big)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\Big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\Big)Z_{3,m,i}-\frac{C_{1}(T)(1\!-\!Kp(T))}{D-K}Z_{4,m,i}\bigg)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{3,m,i}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{5,m,i}^{(T)}\geq 0\}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\Big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\Big)Z_{3,m,i}-\frac{C_{1}(T)(1\!-\!Kp(T))}{D-K}Z_{4,m,i}\bigg)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{3,m,i}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{4,m,i}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{5,m,i}^{(T)}\geq 0\}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\Big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\Big)Z_{3,m,i}-\frac{C_{1}(T)(1\!-\!Kp(T))}{D-K}Z_{4,m,i}\bigg)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{3,m,i}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{4,m,i}\geq 0\}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\frac{\big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\big)^{2}}{4}\mathbb{E}[Z_{3,m,i}^{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\frac{C_{1}(T)^{2}(1-Kp(T))^{2}}{4(D-K)^{2}}\mathbb{E}[Z_{4,m,i}^{2}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\frac{C_{1}(T)\big|\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\big|(1-Kp(T))}{D-K}\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{3,m,i}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{3,m,i}\geq 0\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{4,m,i}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{4,m,i}\geq 0\}}\big]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}DC_{1}(T)^{2}(1-Kp(T))^{2}}{8(D-K)}

\displaystyle-\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}DC_{1}(T)\big|\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\big|(1-Kp(T))\sqrt{K(D-K)}}{2\pi(D-K)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}DC_{1}(T)(1-Kp(T))^{2}}{2(D-K)}\bigg(\frac{C_{1}(T)}{4}-\frac{2\sqrt{K(D-K)}}{\pi D}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}D(1-Kp(T))^{2}}{16(D-K)}\geq\underline{c}\frac{KD^{4}}{\eta(T-T^{*})},

where the last inequality holds by applying the lower bound of
1-Kp(T)
demonstrated in Lemma
D.18
. This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
3.2
and discussion of the worst case example

In this section, we provide a complete proof for Theorem
3.2
, and a worst-case example can attain the upper bound in Theorem
3.2
. We first prove Theorem
3.2
in the following.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

We first upper bound the OOD loss by the sum of three terms as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{OOD}}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})+f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}),f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\rangle\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]},

where the last inequality holds by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Based on this decomposition, it is critical to derive the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]
and
\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]
. For the first term
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}\|_{F}^{2}\big]+2\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big].

By the assumption that each column of
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}
satisfying that
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\xi
, it is straightforward that
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}\|_{F}^{2}\big]\leq D\xi
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big[f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\big]_{m,i}^{2}\Big]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\frac{\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K}\mathbb{E}[\langle\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}/\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in G^{i}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}]\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{K}\leq D\xi\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

For the second term
\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\|_{F}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\Big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\Big)\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle+\frac{C_{1}(T)\big(1-Kp(T)\big)}{D-K}\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\|\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\bigg)^{2}\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad+D\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\|\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{C_{1}(T)^{2}\big(1-Kp(T)\big)^{2}}{(D-K)^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\|_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{KD^{5}\xi}{\eta(T-T^{*})}\bigg).

Here the first inequality holds by
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|\leq|x-y|
. The second inequality is established by the fact
(\sum_{i=1}^{D}a_{i})^{2}\leq D\sum_{i=1}^{D}a_{i}^{2}
for all scalar
a_{i}
’s and
\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\|_{2}^{2}
. Lastly, the third inequality is derived by replacing the conclusions in Lemma
D.18
.
Combining all these derived terms into the three terms derived as the upper bound for OOD loss, we have,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{OOD}}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(D^{3}\xi\sqrt{\frac{K\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{\eta(T-T^{*})}}+\frac{KD^{5}\xi}{\eta(T-T^{*})}\bigg).

Let the upper bound derived above smaller than
\epsilon
, we can derive that

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon}=T^{*}+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{KD^{6}\xi^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{\eta\epsilon^{2}}\bigg)=\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{KD^{6}\xi^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{\eta\epsilon^{2}}\bigg).

This completes the proof.
∎

In the next, we discuss the construction of the worst case
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}
, such that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{OOD}}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T_{\epsilon})};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T_{\epsilon})})-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]\geq\epsilon
for some
T_{\epsilon}=\Theta\big(\frac{MKD^{6}}{\eta\epsilon^{2}}\big)
(assuming
\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}=1
and
\xi=\Theta(1)
for simplicity). In fact, this
T_{\epsilon}
can be different with the
T_{\epsilon}
defined in Theorem
3.2
, but at the same order w.r.t.
\epsilon
, hence a matching result.

By the conclusions in Lemma
D.18
, we know that
\frac{1}{K}-p(T)=\Theta(\frac{D^{2}}{\sqrt{\eta KMT}})
and
\big|p(T)C_{1}(T)-\frac{1}{K}\big|\leq\mathcal{O}(\frac{D}{\sqrt{\eta KMT}})
. Therefore, there exists an absolute constant
c^{\prime}
such that
\frac{1-Kp(T)}{|p(T)C_{1}(T)-\frac{1}{K}|}\geq c^{\prime}D
. In addition, we let
A_{m,i}
to denote the event such that
|\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle|\geq\max\{\frac{2}{c^{\prime}}|\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle|,1\}
. We can assume the probability of
A_{m,i}
is larger than an absolute constant. In fact, such an assumption can be easily verified on many specific distributions like Gaussian distributions. With these notations in hand, we can design
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}
such that its
(m,i)
-th entry is generates as
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{m,i}=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{m,i}\}}+f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})_{m,i}
. Given this construction, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}),f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T)})\rangle\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big(\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}_{m,i}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})_{m,i}\Big)\bigg(\Big(\frac{1}{K}-C_{1}(T)p(T)\Big)\sum_{i_{1}\in G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{1}(T)\big(1-Kp(T)\big)}{D-K}\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{C_{1}(T)\big(1-Kp(T)\big)}{D-K}\Big|\sum_{i_{1}\notin G^{i}}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Big|\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{m,i}\}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{D^{3}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{MK}{\eta T}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{\{A_{m,i}\}}]=\Theta\bigg(D^{3}\sqrt{\frac{MK}{\eta T}}\bigg).

Replacing the
T
with
T_{\epsilon}=\Theta\big(\frac{MKD^{6}}{\eta\epsilon^{2}}\big)
, we can finally conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{OOD}}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T_{\epsilon})};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T_{\epsilon})})-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})\|_{F}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}),f^{*}(\widetilde{\mathbf{X}})-\mathrm{TF}(\widetilde{\mathbf{Z}};\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(T_{\epsilon})};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(T_{\epsilon})})\rangle\big]\geq\Theta\bigg(D^{3}\sqrt{\frac{MK}{\eta}}\frac{\epsilon}{D^{3}}\sqrt{\frac{\eta}{MK}}\bigg)=\Theta(\epsilon).

This validates that the upper bound is indeed attained under our construction.

Appendix F
Technical lemmas

In this section, we present and prove the technical lemmas we used in the proof of the previous sections.

F.1
Calculation details of expectations

We introduce the details regarding

Lemma F.1

(Calculation of
F_{1}(a)
defined in (
D.2
))
.

Let
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)]=a.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)]=\frac{a}{2}.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)]=\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2}.

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.1
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward. When
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function, we can rewrite that
x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)=x\cdot x\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}=x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}
. Therefore, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)]=\mathbb{E}[x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}[x^{2}]}{2}=\frac{a}{2}.

Besides, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, we can rewrite that
x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)=x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}+\kappa^{2}x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x<0\}}
. Therefore, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x\sigma(x)\sigma^{\prime}(x)]=\mathbb{E}[x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}]+\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}[x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x<0\}}]=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}\mathbb{E}[x^{2}]}{2}=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{2},

which finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.2

(Calculation of
F_{2}(a,b)
defined in (
D.3
))
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
be two independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=a.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{a}{2}.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2}.

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.2
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward. For the next two cases, we first introduce some definitions.
Let
x_{3}=x_{1}+x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a+b)
. Then we have
\mathrm{Cov}(x_{1},x_{3})=\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})x_{1}]=a
, and
\mathbb{E}[x_{1}|x_{3}]=\frac{a}{a+b}x_{3}
. Consequently, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}\geq 0\}}]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}\geq 0\}}|x_{3}]\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{a+b}\mathbb{E}[x_{3}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}\geq 0\}}]=\frac{a}{2(a+b)}\mathbb{E}[x_{3}^{2}]=\frac{a}{2}.

In addition, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}\geq 0\}}]+\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}<0\}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}\geq 0\}}|x_{3}]\big]+\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}<0\}}|x_{3}]\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{a+b}\mathbb{E}[x_{3}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}\geq 0\}}]+\frac{\kappa^{2}a}{a+b}\mathbb{E}[x_{3}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{3}<0\}}]=\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.3
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
be two independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=a.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{a}{4}+\frac{a}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{a+b}\bigg).

(F.1)

And there exist the following matching lower and upper bounds:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{a}{4}+\frac{a\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}\right)\vee\left(\frac{a}{2}-\frac{b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]\leq\frac{a}{2}.

(F.2)

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}a}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{a+b}\bigg).

(F.3)

And there exist the following matching lower and upper bounds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]\leq\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2};

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]\geq\bigg(\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}a\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}\bigg)\vee\bigg(\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2}-\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}\bigg).

(F.4)

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.3
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward.
When
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, we can rewrite that
x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})=x_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}
. Let
z_{1}=\frac{x_{1}}{\sqrt{a}}
and
z_{2}=\frac{x_{2}}{\sqrt{b}}
, then we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=a\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]}_{I}.

(F.5)

For
I
, by denoting
\lambda=\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\lambda z_{1}}^{\infty}z_{1}^{2}\phi(z_{1})\phi(z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}^{2}\Phi\left(\lambda z_{1}\right)\phi(z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1},

where
\phi(\cdot)
and
\Phi(\cdot)
are the cumulative distribution function (c.d.f.) and probability density function (p.d.f.) for the standard Gaussian distribution respectively. We can denote that
I(\lambda)=\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}^{2}\Phi\left(\lambda z_{1}\right)\phi(z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1}
. Then, by the Leibniz integral rule, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}I(\lambda)}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}=\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}^{3}\phi(\lambda z_{1})\phi(z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi(1+\lambda^{2})^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{3}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{\pi(1+\lambda^{2})^{2}}.

Additionally, since
I(0)=\frac{1}{4}
, we can derive that

\displaystyle I=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\lambda+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda^{2}}\bigg)=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{a+b}\bigg)

(F.6)

Applying the result of (
F.6
) into (
F.5
), we finishes the proof of (
F.1
). In the next, we derive the upper and lower bound for
I_{1}
. By the property of c.d.f., we know that
\Phi(z)\leq 1
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
, which implies that

\displaystyle I\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}^{2}\phi(z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}]=\frac{1}{2}.

Additionally, by Mills ratio, we further obtain
1-\Phi(z)\leq\phi(z)/z
for all
z>0
. Based on this result, we can obtain that

\displaystyle I\geq\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}^{2}\phi(z_{1})\left(1-\frac{\phi(\lambda z_{1})}{\lambda z_{1}}\right)\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}\phi(z_{1})\phi(\lambda z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1},

where the second term can be calculated by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}\phi(z_{1})\phi(\lambda z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}e^{-\frac{z_{1}^{2}(1+\lambda^{2})}{2}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi(1+\lambda^{2})}\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}e^{-\frac{z_{1}^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi(1+\lambda^{2})}.

Plugging this result into the preceding inequality, we can derive that

\displaystyle I\geq\frac{1}{2}-\frac{b^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2\pi\sqrt{a}(a+b)}.

Combining all these results and (
F.6
), we finally conclude that

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}\bigg)\vee\bigg(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{b^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2\pi\sqrt{a}(a+b)}\bigg)\leq I\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(F.7)

Applying the result of (
F.7
) into (
F.5
), we finishes the proof of (
F.2
). In addition, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, we can similarly derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]

\displaystyle=a\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]+a\kappa\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]

\displaystyle\quad+a\kappa\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}<0\}}]+a\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}<0\}}]

\displaystyle=(1+\kappa^{2})a\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]+2\kappa a\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}],

where the last equality holds by the symmetry of
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
. By applying a very similar calculation process, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[z_{1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]=\int_{-\infty}^{0}z_{1}^{2}\Phi\left(\lambda z_{1}\right)\phi(z_{1})\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{a+b}\bigg).

By replacing this result into the previous calculation, we can immediately prove (
F.3
). And (
•
‣
F.3
) can be directly derived from (
F.2
).
∎

Lemma F.4
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
be two independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=0.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}.

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.4
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward.
When
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, we can rewrite that
x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})=x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}
. Let
z_{1}=\frac{x_{1}}{\sqrt{a}}
and
z_{2}=\frac{x_{2}}{\sqrt{b}}
, then we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\sqrt{ab}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]}_{I}.

(F.8)

For
I
, by denoting
\lambda=\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}
, it can be calculated by

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\lambda z_{1}}^{\infty}z_{1}z_{2}\phi(z_{1})\phi(z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}\phi(z_{1})\left(\int_{-\lambda z_{1}}^{\infty}z_{2}\phi(z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}\phi(z_{1})\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\lambda z_{1}}^{\infty}z_{2}e^{-\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}\phi(z_{1})\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\frac{\lambda^{2}z_{1}^{2}}{2}}^{\infty}e^{-z_{2}}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}z_{1}e^{-\frac{z_{1}^{2}(1+\lambda^{2})}{2}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi(1+\lambda^{2})}=\frac{b}{2\pi(a+b)}.

(F.9)

Now applying the results of (
F.1
) into (
F.8
), we finish the proof when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function. In addition, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{ab}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]+\sqrt{ab}\kappa\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]

\displaystyle+\kappa\sqrt{ab}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}<0\}}]+\kappa^{2}\sqrt{ab}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}<0\}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\kappa^{2})\sqrt{ab}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]+2\kappa\sqrt{ab}\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}],

where the last equality holds by the symmetry of
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
. In addition, by a similar calculation process, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[z_{1}z_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sqrt{a}z_{1}+\sqrt{b}z_{2}\geq 0\}}]=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}z_{1}e^{-\frac{z_{1}^{2}(1+\lambda^{2})}{2}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}=-\frac{1}{2\pi(1+\lambda^{2})}=-\frac{b}{2\pi(a+b)}.

Consequently, we can finally obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{(1-2\kappa+\kappa^{2})b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}=\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)},

which finishes the proof.
∎

Then, based on the conclusions of Lemma
F.3
and Lemma
F.4
, we can immediately obtain the following lemma as a corollary.

Lemma F.5

(Calculation of
F_{3}(a,b)
defined in (
D.4
))
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
be two independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=a.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{a}{4}+\frac{a}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi}.

And there exist the following matching lower and upper bounds:

\displaystyle\frac{a}{2}\vee\left(\frac{a}{4}+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]\leq\frac{a}{2}+\frac{b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a+b)}\leq\frac{a}{2}+\frac{b}{4\pi}.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}a}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi}.

And there exist the following matching lower and upper bounds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]\geq\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}a}{2}\!\vee\!\bigg(\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa)^{2}a}{4}\!+\!\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi}\bigg);

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]\leq\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})a}{2}\!+\!\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}b\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi(a\!+\!b)}\leq\frac{(1\!+\!\kappa^{2})a}{2}+\frac{(1\!-\!\kappa)^{2}b}{4\pi}.

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Lemma F.6

(Calculation of
F_{4}(a,b,c)
defined in (
D.5
))
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
,
x_{3}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,c)
be three independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]=a.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]=\frac{a}{4}+\frac{a}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{c}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{a+b+c}\bigg).

And there exist the following matching lower and upper bounds:

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{a}{4}+\frac{a\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}\bigg)\vee\bigg(\frac{a}{2}-\frac{ac^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2\pi\sqrt{a+b}(a+b+c)}\bigg)\leq\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]\leq\frac{a}{2}.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}a}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{c}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{a+b+c}\bigg).

And there exist the following matching lower and upper bounds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\bigg(\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}a\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}\bigg)\vee\bigg(\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2}-\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}ac^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2\pi\sqrt{a+b}(a+b+c)}\bigg);

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]\leq\frac{(1+\kappa^{2})a}{2}.

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.6
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward.
When
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, we can rewrite that
x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})=x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}
. Additionally, let
x_{4}=x_{1}+x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a+b)
and
z=\frac{x_{4}}{\sqrt{a+b}}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Then we have
\mathrm{Cov}(x_{1},x_{4})=\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})x_{1}]=a
, and
\mathbb{E}[x_{1}|x_{4}]=\frac{a}{a+b}x_{4}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}|x_{1},x_{2}]\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[x_{1}x_{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{4}\geq 0\}}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{4}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\bigg|x_{4}\bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{a}{a+b}\mathbb{E}\bigg[x_{4}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{4}\geq 0\}}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{4}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\bigg]=a\mathbb{E}\big[z^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\geq 0\}}\Phi(\lambda z)\big]=a\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{2}\Phi(\lambda z)\phi(z)\mathrm{d}z}_{I},

where
\lambda=\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{c}}
. By the similar process in the proof of Lemma
F.3
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle I=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\lambda+\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda^{2}}\bigg)=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{c}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{a+b+c}\bigg)

and

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}\bigg)\vee\bigg(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{c^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2\pi\sqrt{a+b}(a+b+c)}\bigg)\leq I\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Plugging these results into the previous equation of expectation, we finish the proof when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the ReLU activation function. In addition, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]+\kappa\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]

\displaystyle+\kappa\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}<0\}}]+\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}<0\}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\kappa^{2})\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]+2\kappa\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}].

By utilizing a similar calculation process, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}(x_{1}+x_{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]=

\displaystyle a\int_{-\infty}^{0}z^{2}\Phi(\lambda z)\phi(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{a}{4}-\frac{a}{2\pi}\bigg(\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{c}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{(a+b)c}}{a+b+c}\bigg).

Plugging this result into the previous calculations, we finish the proof. And the upper and lower bounds for Leaky ReLU activation function can be directly derived by comparing the formulas.
∎

Lemma F.7

(Calculation of
F_{5}(a,b,c)
defined in (
D.6
))
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
,
x_{3}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,c)
be three independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]=0.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]=\frac{b\sqrt{a(b+c)}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]=\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}b\sqrt{a(b+c)}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}.

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.7
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward.
When
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, we can rewrite that
x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})=x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}|x_{1},x_{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x_{1}x_{2}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\phi(x_{1})\phi(x_{2})\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}x_{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi a}}e^{-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2a}}\underbrace{\left(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x_{2}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi b}}e^{-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2b}}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\right)}_{I}\mathrm{d}x_{1}

We can utilize the integral by parts to derive that

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{b}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}e^{-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2b}}-\sqrt{\frac{b}{2\pi}}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2b}}\bigg|_{-\infty}^{\infty}+\sqrt{\frac{b}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2b}}\mathrm{d}\Phi\bigg(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{b}{c}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{2b}-\frac{(x_{1}+x_{2})^{2}}{2c}}\mathrm{d}x_{2}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{b}{c}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{(x_{2}+\frac{b}{b+c}x_{1})^{2}}{2\frac{bc}{b+c}}-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2(b+c)}}\mathrm{d}x_{2}=\frac{b}{\sqrt{2\pi(b+c)}}e^{-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2(b+c)}}

Now substitute this result of
I
back into the outer integral for the calculation for expectation, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]

\displaystyle=\frac{b}{2\pi\sqrt{a(b+c)}}\int_{0}^{\infty}x_{1}e^{-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2a}-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2(b+c)}}\mathrm{d}x_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{b}{2\pi\sqrt{a(b+c)}}\frac{a(b+c)}{a+b+c}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{(a+b+c)x_{1}^{2}}{2a(b+c)}}\mathrm{d}\frac{(a+b+c)x_{1}^{2}}{2a(b+c)}

\displaystyle=\frac{b\sqrt{a(b+c)}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}.

This finish the proof when
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function. In addition, when
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{2}\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]+\kappa\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]

\displaystyle+\kappa\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}<0\}}]+\kappa^{2}\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}<0\}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\kappa^{2})\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\geq 0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]+2\kappa\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}].

By applying a similar calculation process, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[x_{1}x_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}<0\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}\geq 0\}}]=\frac{b}{2\pi\sqrt{a(b+c)}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}x_{1}e^{-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2a}-\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{2(b+c)}}\mathrm{d}x_{1}=-\frac{b\sqrt{a(b+c)}}{2\pi(a+b+c)}.

Applying this result, we finish the proof.
∎

Lemma F.8
.

Let
x_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,a)
,
x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,b)
be two independent Gaussian random variables, then it holds that

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is the identity map, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sigma(x_{1})\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})]=a.

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sigma(x_{1})\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{a}{4}+\frac{a}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi}

•

If
\sigma(\cdot)
is Leaky ReLU activation function, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sigma(x_{1})\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}a}{4}+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}a}{2\pi}\arctan\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a}{b}}\bigg)+\frac{(1-\kappa)^{2}\sqrt{ab}}{2\pi}.

(F.10)

Here,
\kappa
is the coefficient of the Leaky ReLU activation function when the input is smaller than 0.

Proof of Lemma
F.8
.

The first conclusion for the identity map is straightforward.
When
\sigma(\cdot)
is ReLU activation function or leaky ReLU activation function, we can utilize the fact that
x\sigma^{\prime}(x)=\sigma(x)
to re-write that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sigma(x_{1})\sigma(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})]=\mathbb{E}[(x_{1}+x_{2})\sigma(x_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(x_{1}+x_{2})].

And this term has already been calculated in Lemma
F.5
. Hence we finish the proof.
∎

F.2
Arithmetic inequalities

Lemma F.9
.

Let
a,b,c
be three positive scalars, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{c}{a+b}\geq\frac{c}{a}-\frac{bc}{a^{2}}

Proof of Lemma
F.9
.

\displaystyle\frac{c}{a+b}-\frac{c}{a}=-\frac{bc}{(a+b)a}\geq-\frac{bc}{a^{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.10
.

Let
a,b,c
be three positive scalars, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{c}{a-b}\leq\frac{c}{a}+\frac{bc}{(a-b)^{2}}

Proof of Lemma
F.10
.

\displaystyle\frac{c}{a-b}-\frac{c}{a}=\frac{bc}{(a-b)a}\leq\frac{bc}{(a-b)^{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

F.3
Sequence iteration bound

The following lemmas characterize the increase of a positive sequence with matching lower and upper bounds. Similar conclusions and proofs can be found in
24
;
5
;
35
;
63
;
62
. We include the proof here for completeness.

Lemma F.11
.

Consider a positive sequence
\{x_{t}\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
satisfying the following iterative rules:

\displaystyle x_{t+1}\geq x_{t}+\eta\cdot c_{1}\cdot{x_{t}}^{q};

\displaystyle x_{t+1}\leq x_{t}+\eta\cdot c_{2}\cdot{x_{t}}^{q},

where
c_{2}\geq c_{1}>0
are positive constants. For any
v>x_{0}
, let
T_{v}
denote the first index
t
such that
x_{t}\geq v
. Then, for any constant
\zeta>0
, the following bounds on
T_{v}
hold:

\displaystyle T_{v}\leq\frac{1+\zeta}{\eta c_{1}x_{0}^{q-1}}+\frac{(1+\zeta)^{q}c_{2}\log(\frac{v}{x_{0}})}{c_{1}},

(F.11)

and

\displaystyle T_{v}\geq\frac{1}{(1+\zeta)^{q}\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q-1}}-\frac{\log(\frac{v}{x_{0}})}{(1+\zeta)^{q-1}}.

(F.12)

Proof of Lemma
F.11
.

To prove the bounds, let
{\mathcal{T}}_{g}
be the first iteration such that
x_{t}\geq(1+\zeta)^{g}x_{0}
. Furthermore, define
g^{*}
as the smallest integer satisfying
(1+\zeta)^{g^{*}}x_{0}\geq v
. This implies

\displaystyle\frac{\log(\frac{v}{x_{0}})}{\log(1+\zeta)}\leq g^{*}<\frac{\log(\frac{v}{x_{0}})}{\log(1+\zeta)}+1.

For
t={\mathcal{T}}_{1}
, we use the lower bound iteration:

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}\geq x_{0}+\sum_{t=0}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}-1}\eta c_{1}x_{t}^{q}\geq x_{0}+{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\eta c_{1}x_{0}^{q},

from which we can deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\leq\frac{x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}-x_{0}}{\eta c_{1}x_{0}^{q}}.

(F.13)

Utilizing the upper-bound iteration for
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}
and the condition
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}-1}\leq x_{0}(1+\zeta)
, we get

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}\leq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}-1}+\eta c_{2}x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}-1}^{q}\leq x_{0}(1+\zeta)+\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q}(1+\zeta)^{q}.

(F.14)

Combining the results from (
F.13
) and (
F.14
) leads to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\leq\frac{\zeta}{\eta c_{1}x_{0}^{q-1}}+\frac{(1+\zeta)^{q-1}c_{2}}{c_{1}}.

The case for
g>1
is handled similarly. Using the lower bound iteration from
{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}
to
{\mathcal{T}}_{g}-1
:

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}\geq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}+\sum_{t={\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}-1}\eta c_{1}x_{t}^{q}\geq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}+\eta c_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{g}-{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1})x_{0}^{q}(1+\zeta)^{q(g-1)},

(F.15)

and the difference
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}-x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}
can be upper bounded using the upper bound iteration and
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}-1}\leq x_{0}(1+\zeta)^{g}
and
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}\geq x_{0}(1+\zeta)^{g-1}
:

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}-x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}\leq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}-1}+\eta c_{2}x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}^{q}-x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}\leq\zeta(1+\zeta)^{g-1}x_{0}+\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q}(1+\zeta)^{gq}.

(F.16)

Combining (
F.15
) and (
F.16
), we derive that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{g}\leq{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}+\frac{\zeta}{\eta c_{1}x_{0}^{q-1}(1+\zeta)^{(g-1)(q-1)}}+\frac{(1+\zeta)^{q}c_{2}}{c_{1}}.

(F.17)

Taking a telescoping sum of the results of (
F.17
) from
g=1
to
g=g^{*}
and by the fact that
T_{v}\leq{\mathcal{T}}_{g^{*}}
, we finally get (
F.11
). For the lower bound, we proceed similarly starting with
t={\mathcal{T}}_{1}
. We use the upper bound iteration:

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}\leq x_{0}+\sum_{t=0}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}-1}\eta c_{2}x_{t}^{q}\leq x_{0}+{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q}(1+\zeta)^{q}.

Substitute that
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}-x_{0}\geq\zeta x_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\geq\frac{\zeta}{\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q-1}(1+\zeta)^{q}}.

(F.18)

A similar derivation for
g>1
using the upper bound iteration gives:

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}\leq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}+\sum_{t={\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}-1}\eta c_{2}x_{t}^{q}\leq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}+\eta c_{2}({\mathcal{T}}_{g}-{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1})x_{0}^{q}(1+\zeta)^{gq}.

(F.19)

The difference
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}-x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}
can also be lower bounded by utilizing the fact that
x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}-1}\leq x_{0}(1+\zeta)^{g-1}
:

\displaystyle x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}-x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}}\geq x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g}}-x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}-1}-\eta c_{2}x_{{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}-1}^{q-1}\geq\zeta(1+\zeta)^{g-1}x_{0}-\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q}(1+\zeta)^{(g-1)q}.

(F.20)

Combining the results from (
F.19
) and (
F.20
), we obtain that,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{g}\geq{\mathcal{T}}_{g-1}+\frac{\zeta}{\eta c_{2}x_{0}^{q-1}(1+\zeta)^{g(q-1)+1}}-\frac{1}{(1+\zeta)^{q}}.

(F.21)

Taking a telescoping sum of the results of (
F.21
) from
g=1
to
g=g^{*}-1
and by the fact that
T_{v}\geq{\mathcal{T}}_{g^{*}-1}
, we finally get (
F.12
).
∎

Lemma F.12
.

Let
x_{t}
be a positive sequence for
t\geq 0
. Assume
x_{t}
satisfies the iterative formula

\displaystyle x_{t+1}=x_{t}+c_{1}e^{-c_{2}x_{t}}

for given constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. Then, for all
t\geq 0
, the sequence
x_{t}
is bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(c_{1}c_{2}t+e^{c_{2}x_{0}})\leq x_{t}\leq c_{1}e^{-c_{2}x_{0}}+\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(c_{1}c_{2}t+e^{c_{2}x_{0}}).

Proof of Lemma
F.12
.

First, we establish the lower bound for
x_{t}
. We introduce a continuous-time sequence
\underline{x}_{t}
,
t\geq 0
defined by the integral equation with the same initial value.

\displaystyle\underline{x}_{t}=\underline{x}_{0}+c_{1}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c_{2}\underline{x}_{\tau}}\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad\underline{x}_{0}=x_{0}.

(F.22)

Observe that
\underline{x}_{t}
is clearly an increasing function of
t
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\underline{x}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underline{x}_{t}+c_{1}\cdot\int_{t}^{t+1}e^{-c_{2}\underline{x}_{\tau}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\underline{x}_{t}+c_{1}\cdot\int_{t}^{t+1}e^{-c_{2}\underline{x}_{t}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\underline{x}_{t}+c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}\underline{x}_{t})

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.
By comparing the preceding inequality with the iterative formula for
\{x_{t}\}
, the comparison theorem implies that
x_{t}\geq\underline{x}_{t}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Equation (
F.22
) possesses an exact solution given by

\displaystyle\underline{x}_{t}=\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(c_{1}c_{2}t+e^{c_{2}x_{0}}).

Thus, we have

\displaystyle x_{t}\geq\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(c_{1}c_{2}t+e^{c_{2}x_{0}})

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
. This concludes the derivation of the lower bound.

Next, we derive the upper bound for
x_{t}
. We have

\displaystyle x_{t}

\displaystyle=x_{0}+c_{1}\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t-1}e^{-c_{2}x_{\tau}}

\displaystyle\leq x_{0}+c_{1}\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}e^{-\log(c_{1}c_{2}\tau+e^{c_{2}x_{0}})}

\displaystyle=x_{0}+c_{1}\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{t}\frac{1}{c_{1}c_{2}\tau+e^{c_{2}x_{0}}}

\displaystyle=x_{0}+\frac{c_{1}}{e^{c_{2}x_{0}}}+c_{1}\cdot\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}\frac{1}{c_{1}c_{2}\tau+e^{c_{2}x_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq x_{0}+\frac{c_{1}}{e^{c_{2}x_{0}}}+c_{1}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{c_{1}c_{2}\tau+e^{c_{2}x_{0}}}\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the second inequality utilizes the lower bound for
x_{t}
derived in the first part of the lemma’s result. Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{t}

\displaystyle\leq x_{0}+\frac{c_{1}}{e^{c_{2}x_{0}}}+\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(c_{1}c_{2}t+e^{c_{2}x_{0}})-\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(e^{c_{2}x_{0}})

\displaystyle=c_{1}e^{-c_{2}x_{0}}+\frac{1}{c_{2}}\log(c_{1}c_{2}t+e^{c_{2}x_{0}}).

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of the case when
D=K

In this section, we provide the theoretical results for the special case
D=K
. Under this setting, the ground-truth softmax scores reduce to a trivial rank-one structure that
\mathbf{S}^{*}=\frac{1}{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}^{\top}
. Consequently, the initialization
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(0)}=\mathbf{0}_{D\times D}
already yields
\mathbf{S}^{(0)}=\frac{1}{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}^{\top}
, achieving an exact recovery of
\mathbf{S}^{*}
at the start of training. As a result, the gradient with respect to
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}
remains zero throughout the optimization, and the problem effectively reduces to optimizing the single parameter matrix
\mathbf{W}_{V}
. Under this reduced setting, the loss becomes strongly convex in
\mathbf{W}_{V}
, and gradient descent enjoys a linear convergence rate, which is much faster than the
\Theta(1/T)
rate established in Theorem
3.1
. Since Theorem
3.1
provides matching upper and lower bounds and is therefore tight and can not be improved, this linear convergence phenomenon is exclusive to the degenerate case
D=K
. This explains why the proof strategy for Theorem
3.1
does not extend to the
D=K
setting.

Now, we present the following Theorem
G.1
to characterize the loss convergence when a one-layer transformer is supervised by a teacher model
f^{*}(\cdot)
with
\mathbf{S}^{*}=\frac{1}{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}^{\top}
.

Theorem G.1
.

Suppose that
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}
, then for any
t>0
, the excess loss is minimized as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)},\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{opt}}\leq\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}e^{-\eta(t-1)}.

Before we provide the proof for Theorem
G.1
, we first provide and prove the following lemma.

Lemma G.2
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
3.1
, there exist a time dependent non-negative scalar
C(t)
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)}=C(t)\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\ \mathrm{for\ all}\ m\in[M];

(G.1)

and
C(t)
has the following closed formulation:

\displaystyle C(t)=\big(1-\eta F_{1}(1)\big)^{t-1},

where the function
F_{1}(\cdot)
is defined in (
D.2
).
In addition,
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}
remains zero throughout the training.

Proof of Lemma
G.2
.

W.L.O.G., we assume that
\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}
is already normalized, and
\bm{\Gamma}_{m}=[\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{m,2},\ldots,\bm{\xi}_{m,d}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be an orthogonal matrix with
\mathbf{v}_{m}
being its first column.
We prove this lemma by induction. Since these two conclusions holds at initialization with
C(0)=0
. It is sufficient to prove that
\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{V,m}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})=c(t)\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}
and
\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})=\mathbf{0}
, when assuming
\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)}=C(t)\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}
and
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}=\mathbf{0}
. Notice that
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}=\mathbf{0}
implies that
\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i^{\prime},i}=1/D
for all
i^{\prime},i\in[D]
.
By the gradient calculations demonstrated in Lemma
D.1
,
we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{V,m}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg[\mathbf{Y}_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\bigg)\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{S}^{(t)}_{i_{1},i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=-\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Y}_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\frac{\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}}{D}\Bigg]}_{I_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\Bigg]}_{I_{2}}

(G.2)

For
I_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Y}_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\frac{\bm{\Gamma}_{m}\bm{\Gamma}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}}{D}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\frac{\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\Bigg]\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{k=2}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{m,k},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\Bigg]\cdot\bm{\xi}_{m,k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\frac{\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\Bigg]\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}.

The first quality holds as
\mathcal{E}
is mean-zero and independent with
\mathbf{X}
, and the last equality holds as the orthogonality between
\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}
and
\bm{\xi}_{m,k}
implies that
\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{2}}\rangle
is independent with
\langle\bm{\xi}_{m,k},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle
for all
i_{1},i_{2}\in[D]
. Notice that
\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}=\frac{1}{D}\sigma\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\big)
and
\sigma^{\prime}\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\big)=\sigma^{\prime}\big(\frac{C(t)}{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\big)=\sigma^{\prime}\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\big)
. Consequently,
\langle\bm{\xi}_{m,k},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle
is a mean-zero Gaussian random variable, and independent with both
\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\big)
simultaneously, implying that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\langle\bm{\xi}_{m,k},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\Bigg]\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{\xi}_{m,k},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle]=0.

Based on previous results, by plugging
\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}=\frac{1}{D}\sigma\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\big)
and utilizing the definition of
F_{1}(a)
in (
D.2
), we can further derive that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{D}\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\frac{\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\Bigg]\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Bigg]\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}=F_{1}(1)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}.

The second equality is derived by fact that
\sigma(ax)=a\sigma(x)
and
\sigma^{\prime}(ax)=\sigma^{\prime}(x)
if
a\geq 0
. Then we can conclude the final result by the definition of
F_{1}(a)
in (
D.2
). Similar to the process of handling
I_{1}
, we have the following for
I_{2}
:

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\bm{\Gamma}_{m}\bm{\Gamma}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{C(t)}{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Bigg]\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}=C(t)F_{1}(1)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}.

Plugging the calculation results for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
into (
G
), we can immediately derive that
\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{V,m}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})=c(t)\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}^{*}
, which, as we stated previously, directly conclude (
G.1
). In addition, we can further calculate that

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=C(t+1)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m}=\Big(C(t)+\eta F_{1}(1)\big(1-C(t)\big)\Big)\cdot\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},

which implies
C(t)
possesses the updating rules as:

\displaystyle C(t+1)=C(t)+\eta F_{1}(1)\big(1-C(t)\big).

Subtracting
1
on both sides of the equation above and rearranging the terms, we can obtain that

\displaystyle 1-C(t+1)=\big(1-\eta F_{1}(1)\big)\big(1-\eta C(t)\big)=\ldots=\big(1-\eta F_{1}(1)\big)^{t}\big(1-\eta C(0)\big)=\big(1-\eta F_{1}(1)\big)^{t}.

This proves the closed formulation of
C_{1}(t)
. In the next, we prove that
\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})=\mathbf{0}
. By Lemma
D.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{D}\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{D^{2}}\!\!\sum_{m=1}^{M}\!\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\!-\!\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\bigg]\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\!\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle(\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\!-\!\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}})\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{5}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underbrace{\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{6}}.

(G.3)

In the next, we discuss the value of
I_{3}
,
I_{4}
,
I_{5}
, and
I_{6}
respectively. For
I_{3}
, it can be calculated as

\displaystyle I_{3}=

\displaystyle C(t)\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Bigg]\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C(t)}{D}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Bigg]\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle MC(t)F_{1}(1)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}.

The second equation holds as
\mathbb{E}\Big[\sigma\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\big)\sigma^{\prime}\big(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\big)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Big]
takes identical value for all
i_{1}\in[D]
as they follows the same distribution. Similarly, for
I_{4}
, we can calculate it as

\displaystyle I_{4}=

\displaystyle\frac{C(t)}{D}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\Bigg]\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle MC(t)F_{1}(1)\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}.

This implies that
I_{3}=I_{4}
. Through a similar calculation, we can also get

\displaystyle I_{5}=I_{6}=MC^{2}(t)F_{1}(1)\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbf{p}_{i}^{\top}.

Plugging the results that
I_{3}=I_{4}
, and
I_{5}=I_{6}
into (
G
), we immediately concludes that
\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{KQ}}\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})=\mathbf{0}
. This completes the proof.
∎

With the conclusions of Lemma
G.2
, we are ready to prove Theorem
G.1
.

Proof of Theorem
G.1
.

Since we have demonstrated in Lemma
G.2
that
\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)}=\mathbf{0}
, implying
\mathbf{S}^{(t)}=\frac{1}{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}\mathbf{1}_{D}^{\top}
. We can decompose and simplify the loss as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\mathbf{Y}_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\mathcal{E}\|_{F}^{2}\big],

where the last term is essential
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}
, and the last inequality holds by the independence between
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathcal{E}
and the fact that
\mathcal{E}
is zero-mean. Since this equation holds, in the next, we directly deal with
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}
. By utilizing the fact that
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|\leq|x-y|
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{W}_{V}^{(t)};\mathbf{W}_{KQ}^{(t)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{opt}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\big[f^{*}(\mathbf{X})\big]_{m,i}-\sigma\bigg(\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{V,m}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle}{D}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2D}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\Big(1-C(t)\Big)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\langle\mathbf{v}^{*}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}}\rangle\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]=\frac{\big(1-C(t)\big)^{2}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}.

Notice that we have derived
1-C(t)=(1-\eta F_{1}(1))^{t-1}\leq e^{-\eta F_{1}(1)(t-1)}\leq e^{-\eta(t-1)/2}
in Lemma
G.2
, where the last inequality holds as
F_{1}(1)\geq\frac{1}{2}
demonstrated by Lemma
F.1
. Plugging this result into the upper bound above, then we complete the proof.

∎

Appendix H
Additional experiments

In this section, we present additional experimental results on transformer learning of bilinear teacher models under more general training data distributions.

Each batch of training data
{(\mathbf{X}_{n},\mathbf{Y}_{n})}_{n=1}^{N}
is generated with
\mathbf{X}_{n}
drawn from either
(i). a Student-t distribution with
df=5
; or
(ii). a mean-centered Gumbel distribution with
loc=0
and
scale=1
. We then repeat the learning experiments for the six types of teacher models described in Section
4
. Except for the change in the input data distribution, all other configurations remain identical to those in the Gaussian-data experiments.

The results are demonstrated in the following Figures
6
,
7
,
8
, and
9
. Figure
6
and
7
report the results when training data are generated from Student-T distribution, while Figure
8
and
9
report the results when training data are generated from mean-centered Gumbel distribution. We could observe that all these results seems almost identical to those demonstrated in main body. Specifically, for both different distributed training data, we can still observe that the curves of training loss have slopes approximately
-1
on their tails, and the curves of O.O.D. loss have slopes approximates
-0.5
. These results empirically shows that the
\Theta(1/T)
convergence rate for training loss and
\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{T})
convergence rate for O.O.D. loss still hold, even the model are trained on Gaussian data. In addition, we can also observe that the trained softmax attention scores
\mathbf{S}^{(T)}
perfectly replicate the patterns of
\mathbf{S}^{*}
, almost identical to the results obtained on Gaussian data.

\mathbf{W}_{V}
of one layer transformer (
2.4
), and ground truth value matrix
\mathbf{V}^{*}
. These results are presented for experiments where training data is generated from Student-T distribution.

when the training loss converges. These results are presented for experiments where training data is generated from Student-T distribution.

), and ground truth value matrix

. These results are presented for experiments where training data is generated from mean-centered Gumbel distribution.

when the training loss converges. These results are presented for experiments where training data is generated from mean-centered Gumbel distribution.
